No amount of added CI closes it — the fence must be local, because its input set is the working tree *including what git has not been told about*. **Three instances in one review**, which makes it a class, not a coincidence: `055_pfin_etl_role.sql` fully untracked (`??`) while CI ran fully green — a merge would have shipped a deployment runbook linking to a migration that did not exist, and an ETL with no database login identity; the paired QA RLS battery staged-then-modified (`AM`) — a commit would have shipped an older battery than QA verified; the deployment runbook `MM`. Related standing memory: `feedback_verify_paired_artifacts_before_push`.
- **AC.** A pre-push hook (Husky, alongside the existing locked lint + test + type-check + svelte-check + ruff + hadolint set) failing closed on offending paths from `git status --porcelain --untracked-files=all`. Per DevOps decision rules a new fence is a present-options decision and needs a catch criterion **and** a test proving it catches. **Design questions to settle at implementation (record; do not pre-decide):** (i) **must `AM`/`MM` fail as well as `??`** — the QA-battery case was `AM`, so untracked-only would have caught one of three; this is the question that decides coverage; (ii) scope — `supabase/` + `workers/` only, or whole-tree with an ignore list; (iii) an escape hatch for deliberate scratch files that does not make the fence trivially bypassable; (iv) a **paired test proving the fence fails on a real violation** — a fence whose pass condition can be satisfied without the discipline holding is theater.
- **Dependencies.** Upstream: **none — there is no Husky pre-push set.** This line previously named one; `git ls-tree -r main .husky/` returns exactly one entry, `pre-commit`, so this item CREATES the pre-push hook rather than extending an existing one. ⚠ **Two adjacent facts to establish at implementation rather than inherit from this entry, both bearing on its size:** (i) the AC's parenthetical describes the pre-commit set as *"the existing locked lint + test + type-check + svelte-check + ruff + hadolint set"*; as merged, only **ruff** and **hadolint** are wired — `svelte-check` and `eslint` appear in that file **only inside comments** deferring them until SvelteKit scaffolds, and lint/test/type-check are absent. **The AC is left unedited deliberately, because correcting it is a re-scope rather than a dependency fix** — but do not size this item against a set that is largely commentary. (ii) Husky was **not installed** in the checkout until `core.hooksPath` was armed (2026-08-11), so the pre-commit set has no run history to extend; treat *"the hook fires at all"* as something this item verifies, not assumes. Owner: **DevOps**. Intended project Platform / Cross-cutting; intended labels `role:devops` + `surface:ci`, **neither of which exists in the workspace yet**. Non-blocking for the SELF-214 PR. Search-before-create done, no duplicate. Downstream: closes a merge-integrity class CI cannot reach; **S8 is its procedural complement, not a substitute.**

**S5. Dedicated `pfin_provider_sync` login role for `workers/provider-sync` ([ADR-019](DECISIONS.md#adr-019) Condition C2).** [PHASE-7 DEPLOY GATE]
- **Source.** [ADR-041](DECISIONS.md#adr-041) sub-decisions verbatim. SELF-214 gave the ETL a dedicated `pfin_etl` NOINHERIT login role (option (B) ratified over sharing `authenticator`). Two sub-decisions follow: **(i) naming convention `pfin_<workers/ subdirectory>`, effective now** — `workers/provider-sync` → **`pfin_provider_sync`**, which **supersedes the `pfin_worker` name** in ADR-019 C2 (a generic name on the second member of a growing family is how naming drifts; the convention is mechanical and needs no future adjudication). C2's *substance* — a dedicated NOINHERIT role, Sec-joint-review-mandatory on adoption — is **unchanged**; only the name is pinned, and the rename rides C2's existing Sec gate. **(ii) C2 is PROMOTED from "V1.x hardening" to a Phase-7 deploy gate** — an **Architect call on Sec's recommendation**: B8's argument applies **more** strongly to provider-sync than to the ETL it was won for, since provider-sync holds `PLAID_SECRET`, decrypts SD-03 access tokens, and writes the tenant-scoped `account`/`account_trans` surface — the higher-value target, and the one still sharing `authenticator` with PostgREST. Leaving it on the shared credential after ratifying (B) for the lower-value container is not a defensible end-state.
- **AC.** `pfin_provider_sync` created as a NOINHERIT login role (member of `authenticated` + `service_role` only; drops `authenticator`'s inert `anon` membership) and set as provider-sync's `PFIN_DB_USER`, with its password provisioned in the **same Phase-7 deploy pass that switches on `pfin_etl`** — the cluster reaches a consistent role-graph in one operation rather than carrying a half-applied convention across releases. `pfin_etl` and `pfin_provider_sync` stay **deliberately separate, not converged** — collapsing them reintroduces the shared-credential blast radius and loss of independent revocation that option (A) was rejected for. **Sec joint-review-mandatory** (ADR-019 C2's condition, unchanged). Discharges the remainder of **[ADR-023](DECISIONS.md#adr-023) condition C1**: C1's rotation coupling narrowed from three consumers to two at ADR-041 (the ETL is out; PostgREST + provider-sync still bound), and **C1 is fully discharged only when `pfin_provider_sync` replaces `authenticator` in provider-sync's `PFIN_DB_USER`**.
- **Dependencies.** Upstream: `pfin_etl` (`055`) as the shipped precedent + the Phase-7 deploy pass where the credential is first provisioned (per [ADR-021](DECISIONS.md#adr-021) greenfield, it does not exist until deploy). **Explicitly NOT SELF-214 scope.** **Blocked-by:** Phase 7 arrival. Owners: Architect (role migration), DevOps (`secrets-manifest.yml` + `.env.example` + runbook), Sec (joint-review gate).

**S6. V1-scope ruling on the incumbent FMP financial-statement tables (PM + Architect; F/CTO decides).** [scope question — not a defect]
- **Source.** SELF-214 Sec joint-review. Eight `pfin` tables are referenced by `workers/etl/src/pfin_back_etl/core.py` but created by **no V1 migration** — independently verified 2026-08-02 (`create table` grep across `supabase/migrations/`: zero hits for all eight). Incumbent code carried in at the [ADR-019](DECISIONS.md#adr-019) monorepo fold and never reconciled; the path is dead against V1 under any credential and fails loud (`UndefinedTable`), so there is **no security consequence and it blocks nothing**. **THIS IS NOT A GRANTS TASK** — Sec and Backend both initially scoped it as one, and **you cannot `GRANT` on a table that does not exist**. It is a product-scope question, which is why it routes to PM rather than Architect. **PM finding — the eight are NOT homogeneous, and splitting them collapses most of the question:**
  - **Class A — already superseded in V1; the scope question is CLOSED and only dead code remains (2 tables).** `pfin.cpi` → superseded by **`pfin.cpi_u_index`** (`053`, SELF-230), whose own header says it reuses "the proven incumbent BLS CPI fetch logic." `pfin.asset_cat` → superseded by **`pfin.user_taxonomy`** (`009`/`010`) + **`pfin.user_asset_category`** (`022`); `010` explicitly restores the incumbent `asset_cat` `notes` content. For these two the V1 answer already exists — the residue is a **dead-code retirement in `core.py`**, not a product decision.
  - **Class B — the genuine open question (6 tables).** `equity_profile`, `reporting_period`, `income_statement`, `balance_sheet_statement`, `cash_flow_statement`, `earning` — FMP **company-fundamentals** data.
- **AC.** An F/CTO ruling on Class B, framed as **V1 / later-V1.x / V2+ / never**, recorded as an ADR. **PM position (to be argued, not assumed):** **none of the 32 locked [PRD §2](docs/PRD/index.html#sec-2) V1 stories requires company fundamentals** — V1 is net worth, allocation, cash flow, estimated taxes, and the monthly report; fundamentals serve *security analysis*, which sits on the advisor/fiduciary axis that [PRD §6.3](docs/PRD/index.html#sec-6-3) / [ADR-007](DECISIONS.md#adr-007) made a **permanent non-goal** for TLH recommendations. That is a strong prior for **V2+ or never**, but it is **not the same non-goal** and must not be waved through as one — fundamentals could serve a read-only display use with no advice attached. **This is an incumbent-exceeds-V1 decision and must NOT be auto-accepted via "selective adoption"** (memory `feedback_incumbent_exceeds_v1_review`): it gets a P-flag with A/B/C options + an ADR. Class A needs no ruling — only a retirement issue.
- **Dependencies.** Upstream: [ADR-019](DECISIONS.md#adr-019) (the monorepo fold that carried the code in without reconciling it). Owners: **PM** (frames the V1/V2/never options + the PRD §2 trace), **Architect** (migration cost if any class lands in V1; `core.py` retirement shape), **F/CTO** (decides). No Sec gate (no live surface — the path is dead). Downstream: either 6 new tables enter a milestone, or `core.py`'s fundamentals paths are retired and the dead-reference class closes. **⚠ Downstream gate — S24(a) must be met or explicitly ruled unnecessary BEFORE this entry's implementation lands.** The dead calls retired here are the only thing keeping `update_table_all`'s missing exception isolation unreachable: removing the **last** of them arms a CPI-U reconciliation failure to abort `update_table_eod_price` (→ NAV → net worth). **Retiring only Class A does not arm it** — six Class B calls still sit between the CPI-U gate and `eod_price` — so the trigger is the last dead call leaving the sequence, i.e. **Class A retirement plus the Class B ruling, both implemented.** Added 2026-08-10 as the reciprocal half of S24; without it the arming commit's author has no reason to look.

**S7. Discharge [ADR-011](DECISIONS.md#adr-011) Decision 1 clause (d) for the W-1 NAV worker (audit-log deferral).**
- **Source.** [ADR-040](DECISIONS.md#adr-040) verbatim — clause (d) is recorded as **EXPLICITLY DEFERRED, not satisfied, not waived** (Sec joint-review B3, recorded at Sec's requirement). [ADR-011](DECISIONS.md#adr-011) Decision 1 commits non-JWT writes to a four-clause discipline and states a new privileged-context surface **MUST** adopt it at design time; it names **"Lock 7 NAV worker"** under *Confirmed reusability*, so this surface is one of the four D1 was generalized from, not merely covered by it. Clauses (a)/(b)/(c) are satisfied at ship; **(d) explicit audit log capturing the tenant-resolution chain is NOT** — `emit_audit_log` raises `NotImplementedError` and the worker emits no audit row. **ADR-040 states a tracked follow-up is required to carry the unblocking work; this entry is that record.** *(Absent from Sec's ranked six — a ranking-scope artifact, not a de-prioritization; see the section preamble.)*
- **AC.** The W-1 worker emits a same-transaction audit row capturing the **tenant-resolution chain**: run identity, which tenants were enumerated, per-tenant success/failure, and the fact of a checkpoint having been **attempted and failed** (today a tenant that errors leaves no durable trace at all). Scope note — `nav_daily` is itself immutable and already records the tenant-resolution *outcome* (`users_id`, `nav_date`, `nav_value`, `created_at`); what is missing is the *chain*, so **risk is forensic, not exploitative** — which is why this is a recorded deferral rather than a ship-block, and must not be restated as one. Sec joint-review-mandatory (ADR-011 Decision 2 audit-class).
- **Dependencies.** **Blocked-by:** the `plaid_sync_audit`-class audit table **plus** the general same-transaction audit-log insert helper — the **reserved, already-allowlisted 4th DEFINER slot**, still unauthored (its tracker is named in [ADR-040](DECISIONS.md#adr-040) / [ADR-026](DECISIONS.md#adr-026) as the manual-accounts issue's Task #7; the ID is deliberately not repeated here. **⚠ Verified 2026-08-03 against live Linear: that manual-accounts issue is CLOSED (Done 2026-07-06, PRs #141–144), so its "Task #7" is not a workable tracker** — the audit-log helper was deferred *out* of it at close (see [`MILESTONES.md`](MILESTONES.md) V1.0 close notes) and currently has **no live tracker of its own**. That is not a citation typo: the reference is historically accurate about where the work was scoped, and wrong only about whether that scope is still open. Re-derive or create a tracker when the Linear free-issue limit lifts; until then this §7 entry IS the tracker). **Authoring it moves no ledger** — the SECURITY DEFINER allowlist is committed at 4 (authored 3), and the §10 catalogued-instance ledger is separate and unchanged. Owners: Architect (audit table + DEFINER helper), Backend (`workers/etl` emit path), Sec (joint-review).

**S8. Record the working-tree-vs-`main..HEAD` lesson in the `finish-feature` / `merge-pr` skills.** [process control]
- **Source.** Architect proposal at the SELF-214 review, concurred by team-lead and PM. The pre-push-gate lesson — **compare the working tree, not `main..HEAD`** — is a process control, and a **skill** is where someone encounters it at the moment it matters; an ADR is not read at merge time. Compounds the standing memory `feedback_verify_paired_artifacts_before_push`.
- **AC.** Add the check to the `finish-feature` and `merge-pr` skill bodies: before push/PR/merge, verify the **working tree** (`git status --porcelain --untracked-files=all`), not only `git diff --stat main..HEAD` — an untracked or staged-then-modified file is invisible to the latter, which is exactly how `055` nearly merged omitted. **Explicitly subordinate to S4:** the hook is the structural fence, this is the procedural complement. **It must not be treated as a substitute** — a procedural-over-structural trade is what Sec ruled against elsewhere in this review, and if S4 lands this note becomes genuine belt-and-suspenders rather than the false kind S3 is about.
- **Dependencies.** Upstream: none. Owner: **team-lead / DevOps** (skill definitions). Distinct from S4 (the hook itself). Two-way door.

**S9. Record three battery-design lessons in `supabase/tests/rls/DESIGN.md`.** [process control]
- **Source.** QA's request at the SELF-214 review. Distinct from S8 (different file, different owner, different content): S8 is about *what to check before merging*; this is about *how to build a battery that cannot lie to you*. All three are failure modes where a battery reports success it did not earn.
- **AC.** Record, each with the SELF-214 instance that produced it: **(i) leg-independence** — an inversion suite proves the legs are *independent*, not merely that the fences have teeth; a battery that only asserts "fence fires" cannot distinguish one fence doing all the work from each leg holding on its own. **(ii) shape-agnostic assertions degrade silently** — an assertion written loosely enough to survive a pending decision keeps passing after that decision lands, having quietly stopped testing the thing it was written for; the degradation is invisible because the test still goes green. **(iii) the partial-run guard** — **"nothing failed" and "nothing ran" are indistinguishable unless you count.** A battery must assert its own expected assertion count, or a setup error that skips the suite reads as a clean pass. This one generalizes past RLS and is the strongest of the three.
- **Dependencies.** Upstream: the existing two-tenant pgTAP battery + `supabase/tests/rls/`. Owner: **QA** (`supabase/tests/rls/DESIGN.md`). Two-way door. Downstream: **directly strengthens S1** — the C3 invariant assertion is only worth booking if the battery carrying it cannot silently no-op, which is exactly lesson (iii). Sec generalises the point past S1: it holds for **every assertion-type entry in §7** — *"an invariant assertion's entire value is that it goes RED at the right moment; a harness that can abort past it and still report clean makes it **worse than absent** — absent, nobody is reassured; present-but-skippable, everybody is."* S1 is flagged only because "highest value *if it fires*" is the profile that suffers most from a silent no-op.

**S10. ~~Capability-verify `compute_and_checkpoint_user` end-to-end under a real `pfin_etl` login before Phase-7 scheduling.~~ — ✅ DISCHARGED 2026-08-09.** [was: PHASE-7 DEPLOY GATE]
- **✅ Result.** Executed to Sec's AC verbatim. **(i) Authentication proven** — a real `LOGIN` as `pfin_etl` (not `SET ROLE`), **with a negative control**: a wrong password is rejected, so the credential is what authenticated and not the `pg_hba` `127.0.0.1 trust` line. **(ii) Python layer proven** — real `TenantBoundConnection`, `impersonate()`, `_check_tenant_statement` and SQLAlchemy binding. **Run 1 `rowcount = 1`; same-day run 2 `rowcount = 0`** — the AC — with the computed NAV identical across both. Row verified: tenant matches the account owner, `created_at` set, value finite and positive; `nav_date` resolved **UTC** per the `061` pin, one day ahead of local. Fences re-verified **on the real row**: UPDATE and DELETE both blocked by the append-only trigger, and an unbound INSERT still raises `write-tenant binding REJECTED`. Credential revoked (`ALTER ROLE pfin_etl NOLOGIN`) and the secret shredded afterwards.
- **⚠ Deviation, stated rather than buried.** The local Supabase CLI stack offers no TLS while `build_database_url` **hard-coded** `sslmode=require` *(true as written at discharge; **fixed since, at `a85c9f5` — see S11**, which is why this bullet is past-tense now and was not simply deleted)*, so the run patched **that parameter only**, in a scratchpad runner that is **not committed**. Everything else was the production path. **TLS transport is therefore NOT covered by this discharge** — S10 does not ask for it, and the deploy-time §6.1 checks are unchanged. **That half is unchanged by S11's fix and remains true today:** S11 made the parameter configurable, which is a *verifiability* fix; it did not put a TLS-terminating stack under the discharge run, so no TLS transport has ever been exercised end to end by this worker. Do not read S11's discharge as closing it.
- **It found two defects, which is the point.** See **S11** and **S12** — both are the *"nobody had run the assembled path"* class that S10 was written to catch, and both are invisible to static review.
- **Source.** Sec FINAL VERDICT **GREEN, condition 2** (SELF-214). The **B9 defect** — a targeted `ON CONFLICT` failing `42501` on every run under an INSERT-only grant — **existed because nobody had run the assembled statement.** It was invisible to every static review, including Sec's own. **Merging with the assembled path still unrun reproduces the exact condition that produced it.** Instance of the standing discipline `feedback_capability_verify_adr_db_primitives`: ADR/Lock-named DB primitives are **unrun assumptions** until executed under the real production role.
- **AC.** **Sec's ratified wording, verbatim:** *"Before the ETL container is scheduled in Phase 7: execute `compute_and_checkpoint_user` **once, through the Python worker**, **authenticating** as `pfin_etl`, against a real database, and verify a same-day re-run returns `row_count = 0`."* **What is already proven — do not re-litigate:** the SQL *composition*, executed against a live database in a rolled-back transaction — NOINHERIT denies ambient privilege → the impersonated read resolves the correct tenant → the B7 GUC survives teardown → the production statement inserts once and a re-run returns 0. **What is NOT proven, and is this entry's whole point — do not close it early on the strength of the above:** **(i) authentication** as `pfin_etl` — the verification *assumed* the role via `SET ROLE`; production **logs in**, which needs a password and a commit; **(ii) the Python layer** — `impersonate()`, `_check_tenant_statement`, and SQLAlchemy's binding all sit **above** what was executed. **Backend's test suite cannot reach this either: SQLite does not model Postgres grants.** So no existing green signal covers it.
- **Dependencies.** Upstream: the `pfin_etl` credential provisioned per deployment-runbook §6.1 (the `\password` two-step) + `055`. **Not a merge blocker** — nothing is deployed, the migration has never been applied anywhere, the cron is not scheduled — **but it IS a deploy blocker**, and it is better written as one now than discovered as one later. Owners: **DevOps** (credential + Phase-7 sequencing), **Backend** (the worker-path execution), **Sec** (condition-2 closure). Downstream: closes Sec's GREEN condition 2; gates ETL container scheduling.

**S11. ~~`build_database_url` hard-codes `sslmode=require`, so the ETL cannot be run against the local stack at all.~~ — ✅ DISCHARGED 2026-08-09 at `a85c9f5` (PR #355).** [was: defect — blocks local verification]
- **✅ What shipped**, stated as behaviour rather than "fixed": the transport parameter is read from **`<prefix>DB_SSLMODE`** (`PFIN_DB_SSLMODE` for the ETL) in `load_env_variables`, and `build_database_url` resolves it against an **allowlist of libpq's six modes**, **defaulting to `require`**. Unset, absent, and blank all resolve to `require`, so production is unchanged and an environment that simply forgot to set it still demands TLS. **Only an explicit value can weaken the transport**, and an unrecognised one **raises rather than falling back** — a silent fallback would change the transport posture without telling anyone. `"REQUIRE"` is rejected too; libpq is case-sensitive. **The default is the security-load-bearing part, not the override.**
- **Verified against the tree, not inferred from the PR.** `a85c9f5` is an ancestor of `main`, and the allowlist + default are present in `workers/etl/src/pfin_back_etl/utils.py` at `832cfb7`.
- **⚠ This entry sat un-struck across two merges while its own fix was already on `main`**, and S10's Deviation bullet went on asserting the hard-code in the **present tense** for just as long — corrected above. **The filing convention held; the discharge convention did not.** Worth noting because the failure is silent in the direction that matters: a stale open defect makes work look *undone*, so it costs a re-investigation rather than a wrong action — which is exactly why nothing forces its correction.
- **Source.** Found 2026-08-09 while discharging **S10**. `utils.build_database_url` appends `?sslmode=require` unconditionally — no env var, no parameter, no local-dev path — and the local Supabase CLI stack does not offer TLS (`server does not support SSL, but SSL was required`). The `workers/etl` README assumes a *hosted* Supabase instance, which has TLS.
- **⚠ Why this matters more than it looks.** The barrier sits at **connection time, before any logic runs**, so *no part* of the worker can be exercised locally without modifying committed code. **This is plausibly why the assembled path had never run end to end** — the thing S10 exists to correct was itself protected by an obstacle nobody had to name. A verification gate that cannot be run without patching the code under test tends not to get run.
- **AC** *(met, with one residual named rather than rounded up)*. Make the transport parameter configurable — env-driven with `require` as the **default**, so production is unchanged and unspecified environments still fail closed. **Do not** simply relax it: `require` is correct for production and `utils.py` documents TLS as a property of this function. ✅ **Done.** Pair with a note in the `workers/etl` README naming the local-stack path. **⚠ NOT done** — the env plumbing is documented in `workers/etl/.env.example:178–185`, which is arguably the better home, but **the README named in the AC carries no `sslmode` mention at all** (grep-verified at `832cfb7`). Recorded as a **doc-only residual**, not silently counted as met; out of scope for this doc-only sweep, which touches no `workers/` file.
- **Owner.** Backend (the function) + DevOps (env plumbing). **Was not a merge blocker; it was a verifiability blocker** — and clearing it is what let S13 become findable at all.

**S12. ~~The NAV worker refuses to start without two API keys it never uses.~~ — ✅ DISCHARGED 2026-08-09 (PR #369).** [was: defect — Phase-7 deploy consequence]
- **✅ What shipped.** `load_env_variables` split into **`load_db_params()` + `load_api_keys()`**; **the loader raises for nothing**, and `require_api_key()` raises **at the point of use**. `NavDailyWorker` now calls `load_db_params` alone — measured, it makes zero external API calls — so its cron container needs neither key. Chosen over a `require=(...)` argument because a requirement-set parameter keeps one function answering two questions and **forgetting to pass it would silently loosen the check rather than trip it**; with the split, *which credentials does this job need?* is answered by **which function it calls**.
- **⚠ Scope was wider than as filed.** The entry names `NavDailyWorker`; **`PFinBackend` had the same defect plus an unconditional `PFinFMP` construction in `__init__`**, so a CPI-only container had to carry an FMP credential it never uses. The FMP client is now **lazy and memoised**.
- **The AC is enforced in CI, not asserted by hand.** `NavDailyWorker()` constructing with DB parameters alone is a **hermetic unit test** — SQLAlchemy engines are lazy, so no database is touched — and a second test pins that the worker holds **no API credential values at all**, making least-privilege a checked property. ⚠ Bounded: it proves the worker does not **load** the credential; **the process environment is untouched**, and per-container env scoping is DevOps-owned.
- **The two tests asserting the loader RAISES were REPOINTED, not deleted** — the requirement moved rather than vanished, and they now pin where.
- **Source.** Found 2026-08-09 while discharging **S10**. `NavDailyWorker.__init__` calls `utils.load_env_variables`, which **hard-requires `FMP_API_KEY` and `BLS_API_KEY`** and raises `ValueError` when either is absent. **Measured: `nav_daily.py` contains zero references to either key and makes zero external API calls** — it computes NAV from the database through `fn_compute_nav`.
- **⚠ The deploy consequence is concrete.** `secrets-manifest.yml` scopes those two keys to *other* ETL jobs — `FMP_API_KEY` to security-price ingest, `BLS_API_KEY` to CPI-U ingest. So the Phase-7 NAV cron container must either be handed **two credentials it has no business holding** — cutting against least-privilege and the secrets non-overlap commitment — **or it dies on its first scheduled run**, before doing any work. Same shape as the **B9** defect that motivated S10: invisible to every static review, fatal on every run.
- **AC.** Decouple credential loading from worker construction: load only what a given worker needs (or make the API-key requirements opt-in per job), so the NAV checkpoint path constructs with DB parameters alone. Then confirm the Phase-7 cron's secret set contains **no** key it does not use.
- **✅ SHAPE RULED 2026-08-09 (team-lead), so it is not re-litigated at implementation time.** Split `load_env_variables` into **`load_db_params()` + `load_api_keys()`**, with the **loader raising for nothing** and the **key accessor raising at the point of use**. Chosen over a `require=(...)` parameter on the existing function: a requirement-set argument keeps one function answering two questions, and every new caller has to remember to pass the right set — the failure mode being that forgetting it silently loosens the check rather than tripping it. Splitting makes the question *"which credentials does this job need?"* answerable by **which function it calls**, and moving the raise to the point of use keeps the failure **loud and located at the consumer** instead of at construction, which is the property S12 exists to restore.
- **⚠ Scope widened at planning, 2026-08-09.** As filed this entry names `NavDailyWorker`. **`PFinBackend` has the same defect** and it is not a duplicate: `PFinBackend.__init__` additionally constructs `PFinFMP(api_key=self._params["FMP_API_KEY"])` at `core.py:484`, so **a CPI-only container must carry an FMP credential it never uses** — the same least-privilege and secrets-non-overlap objection, on the worker that actually does the CPI-U work. The fix must cover both entry points.
- **Owner.** Backend (the loader) + DevOps + Sec (the manifest scoping). **Deploy blocker for the NAV cron**, not a merge blocker.

**S13. ~~`PFinBackend` cannot be constructed against the V1 schema — SQLAlchemy automap collides on `pfin.user_taxonomy.tax_character`.~~ — ✅ DISCHARGED 2026-08-09 (PR #357).** [was: ⚠ PRODUCTION-FATAL for the ETL entry point]
- **✅ What shipped.** Two automap naming hooks that rename the generated **RELATIONSHIP**, never the column, and only on a real collision — delegating to automap's own default otherwise, so upstream naming stays tracked. **Fixed at the mapper, not the schema:** no migration, no DDL; `tax_character` is ratified domain vocabulary on a locked surface and the defect was in automap's naming. Verified on `main`: `.tax_character` is still the TEXT column and the relationship moved to `.tax_character_ref`.
- **The design point worth keeping:** the fallback name is keyed on the **FK's own columns**, not a constant `_ref` suffix — a constant would collapse multiple FKs from one table to one target onto a single name, **trading S13's loud failure for a silent wrong one**, which is precisely the defect **S15** records and which remains open.
- **Source.** Found 2026-08-09 attempting the SELF-230 CPI-U backfill, immediately after **S11** removed the connection-level barrier that had been hiding it. Constructing `PFinBackend()` raises before any work begins:
  > `sqlalchemy.exc.ArgumentError: when configuring property 'tax_character' on Mapper[user_taxonomy(user_taxonomy)], column 'tax_character' conflicts with property '<_RelationshipDeclared … tax_character>'`
- **Mechanism, measured.** `pfin.user_taxonomy` carries **a column `tax_character` (text)** *and* **an FK `fk_user_taxonomy_tax_character` → table `pfin.tax_character`**. Automap names the generated relationship after the **target table**, which collides with the same-named column, and automap raises rather than disambiguating. Introduced by `009` (`user_taxonomy`) + `011` (`tax_character` registry) — **latent for ~50 migrations.**
- **⚠ Blast radius: the production ETL entry point.** `main.py:48` does `pfb = PFinBackend()` then `pfb.update_table_all()`. **It cannot start.** Every `PFinBackend`/`SBaseConn` method is unreachable — the CPI-U importer, the FMP security-price ingest, and `update_table_all()` itself. **This is not local-only**: automap reflects whatever schema it connects to, and production carries the same migrations.
- **Why it stayed invisible.** `NavDailyWorker` has **zero** automap references — it goes through `TenantBoundConnection` with raw SQL — which is exactly why **S10 passed while this was broken**. The one worker anyone had run was the one that bypasses the defect. Compounded by **S11**: nothing could connect locally at all, so the failure sat one layer behind a barrier nobody could get past.
- **AC.** Disambiguate at the automap layer — `name_for_scalar_relationship` / `name_for_collection_relationship` hooks, or `exclude_properties`, so the relationship does not take the column's name. **Do not rename the column or the table**: `tax_character` is a ratified domain term on a locked surface and the defect is in the mapper, not the schema. Then **construct `PFinBackend()` against a stack at the current migration head as the acceptance test** — that is the check whose absence let this live ~50 migrations, and it belongs in the ETL unit lane where it can run without credentials.
- **Owner.** Backend. **Deploy blocker for the ETL container; blocks SELF-218 (CPI-U overlay) until fixed**, because the importer is unreachable.

**S14. ~~Reconcile the BLS-key requirement: ARCH §5 says keyless, the code hard-requires a key.~~ — ✅ DISCHARGED 2026-08-09 ([ADR-048](DECISIONS.md#adr-048), PR #365): ruled KEYED.** [was: flagged discrepancy — reconcile deliberately]
- **✅ What was ruled.** The requirement is **settled as KEYED**: the importer posts to the BLS **v2** endpoint, which takes a `registrationkey`, so the key is genuinely required by the code as written. **The architecture was corrected to the code, not the reverse** — the reconciled framing now lives in **ARCH §7**, and the discrepancy note in `workers/etl/.env.example` was repointed to it.
- **⚠ Citation repointed §5 → §7 in the LIVE text only.** The reconciled framing lives in **ARCH §7**; `.env.example` was repointed to match, and this entry's Owner line now names §7. ⚠ **The struck title and the Source quotation deliberately still read "§5" — they are a record of what was filed, and §7 never said "keyless".** Repointing a citation inside struck or quoted historical text does not update it, it falsifies it: the struck title would then assert something the cited section never claimed.
- **Rider discharged separately:** the eager `BLS_API_KEY` raise in the shared loader is fixed by **S12** — a missing BLS key can no longer kill FMP or Plaid work, because the raise moved to the point of use.
- **Source.** ⚠ *The quotation below is a VERBATIM record of what `.env.example` said at filing, and its "ARCH §5" is left INTACT deliberately — repointing a citation inside a quotation would make the quotation false. The live file now cites §7; see the repointing bullet above.* The discrepancy was flagged verbatim in `workers/etl/.env.example` and was **not** silently reconciled: *"ARCH §5 frames BLS as 'free/open' (no key), but the incumbent code (utils.py) REQUIRES `BLS_API_KEY` (raises ValueError if absent). The BLS public API accepts an optional registration key that raises rate limits."* Surfaced again 2026-08-09 when the CPI backfill needed a credential the architecture says should not be needed.
- **The substance.** `fetch_cpi_df` posts to BLS **v2**, which requires a `registrationkey`. BLS also publishes a **keyless v1** endpoint with lower limits (fewer requests/day, shorter year span). So the key is a **rate-limit lift, not an access requirement** — and the code's hard `raise` turns an optional optimisation into a hard dependency.
- **⚠ Why it is worth reconciling rather than leaving flagged.** A credential that is not strictly needed still has to be provisioned, rotated, scoped in `secrets-manifest.yml`, and handed to a container — and it forced a **production-classified credential to be borrowed for a local run** (F/CTO-authorised 2026-08-09, but the borrow is exactly what secrets non-overlap exists to avoid). Removing an unnecessary credential dependency is worth more than documenting it.
- **AC** *(discharged via option (b))*. Decide, with ARCH: either (a) make the key **optional** — v1 keyless fallback when `BLS_API_KEY` is absent, v2 when present, with the year-span difference handled — and correct the code's `raise`; or (b) confirm the key is genuinely required for V1's needs and **correct ARCH §5** instead. Either way the artifacts must stop disagreeing. If (a), the backfill's 2015→now span must be checked against v1's shorter per-request year limit.
- **Owner.** Architect (**ARCH §7** — the reconciled framing's home; it was §5 at filing) + Backend (the loader) + Sec (manifest scoping). **Was not blocking** — a dependency-reduction and a doc-vs-code reconciliation, resolved by correcting the architecture to the code rather than the reverse.

**S15. ~~`pfin.lot_match`'s two FKs to `pfin.account_trans` collapse onto one automap relationship — SILENTLY.~~ — ✅ DISCHARGED 2026-08-10 (PR #381).** [was: latent defect — no error, no warning]
- **✅ What shipped.** When a table declares more than one FK to the same target, each relationship now takes a **meaning-carrying name derived from its own FK columns** — `buy_trans` / `sell_trans` rather than a second `account_trans` that silently overwrites the first. **Mapper-attribute rename only: no DDL, no migration, Decision 3 family untouched.**
- **⚠ BOTH SIDES COLLAPSED INDEPENDENTLY, and that is the non-obvious part.** The scalar and the collection are named by separate hooks, so each collapsed on its own — which is why the scalar resolved to the **SELL** leg while its *nominal reverse* collection resolved to the **BUY** leg. **They were not reverses of each other**, and a reader following one got the other's FK. Fixing only the scalar would have left a mapping that still lied, more quietly.
- **The S13 guard correctly declined to act, which is why this needed a separate condition rather than a widening.** That guard fires on a relationship-vs-**column** collision; there is none — `lot_match` has no `account_trans` column. **Nothing was claimed, so nothing looked wrong.**
- **Scope re-measured at the fix, not re-cited:** still exactly **one** `(child → parent)` pair with >1 FK, re-taken with the applied head verified unmoved (62/62, `062`) rather than quoted from the earlier run. **Blast radius was ZERO at the time of fixing** — no ETL source referenced either attribute — which is why it was cheap now and why it was never urgent.
- **The tests construct the generality they claim**, because the live schema exercises the guard exactly once: a three-FK case, a no-colliding-column case, the reverse side asserted separately, and a single-FK non-intervention case. Verified end to end against the real schema, not only through the hooks.
- **Source.** Found 2026-08-09 by Backend while fixing **S13** (PR #357), and deliberately filed rather than fixed: it needs a naming judgment beyond S13's AC, and S13's collision guard **correctly declines to intervene** because there is no relationship-vs-column collision here.
- **Mechanism, measured.** `lot_match` holds **two** FKs to the same target — `lot_match_buy_trans_id_fkey (buy_trans_id)` and `lot_match_sell_trans_id_fkey (sell_trans_id)`, both → `pfin.account_trans` (verified independently at `e387997`). Automap names both after the **referred table**, so they collapse:
  - `lot_match.account_trans` (scalar) joins on **`sell_trans_id`** — **the buy side is unreachable**
  - `account_trans.lot_match_collection` joins on **`buy_trans_id`**
  - **The scalar and its nominal reverse are therefore NOT reverses of each other.**
- **⚠ Why this is arguably worse than S13.** S13 **raised** — loudly, on construction, blocking everything until fixed. This produces **no error and no warning**: the mapping simply silently means something other than it reads. A reader who follows `lot_match.account_trans` expecting "the transaction this match refers to" gets the sell leg with no indication the buy leg exists.
- **Blast radius TODAY is zero, and that is the reason to fix it now rather than later.** No ETL source references either attribute — the only mention of `lot_match` in `workers/etl/src` is an explanatory comment in S13's own guard (grep-verified). So it can be corrected while nothing depends on the wrong meaning; the first consumer to reach for it is the point at which this becomes expensive.
- **AC.** Give the two FKs distinct, meaning-carrying relationship names (`buy_trans` / `sell_trans` or equivalent) rather than letting either take the bare table name — a bare `account_trans` on a two-FK table is the ambiguity, exactly as a bare `is_active` on an account row was at ADR-042 `059`. Pair with an assertion that **both** legs are independently reachable and that each resolves to the FK it names, since the failure mode here is a mapping that is present and wrong rather than absent.
- **✅ Scope MEASURED 2026-08-09 (read-only catalog query, local stack verified at migration head 62/62), because the fix's cost turns entirely on this number.** Across **both** schemas automap reflects (`pfin` + `auth`), **exactly ONE (child → parent) pair carries more than one FK**: `pfin.lot_match → pfin.account_trans` (`lot_match_buy_trans_id_fkey`, `lot_match_sell_trans_id_fkey`). **There is no second instance.** This matters in both directions: the fix **cannot** silently rename relationships on tables nobody was thinking about — the risk that would have made it a large change — **and** the guard it installs is therefore **unexercised by any other pair**, so its test must construct the generality it claims rather than infer it from this one case. Estimate firms at **0.5 PR**. ⚠ The count is a property of the **schema at `062`**, not an invariant: **re-measure, do not re-cite**, once new migrations land.
- **Owner.** Backend. **Not blocking** — latent, zero current consumers.

**S16. ~~The ETL integration lane is now REACHABLE, and what it exposes is pre-existing incumbent-vs-V1 drift.~~ — ✅ ACTIONABLE HALF DISCHARGED 2026-08-09 (PRs #364 + #366); the scope question remains PM + Architect's.** [was: note against an existing scope question — not a new defect]
- **✅ What shipped.** The fixture policy now permits a skip for **exactly one reason — there is no database to talk to**; every other failure propagates. **And the login role is assertable** (`PFIN_TEST_EXPECT_ROLE`, plus `rolsuper`/`rolbypassrls`/`rolinherit` all required FALSE), because narrowing the `except` makes a failure *louder* without making one *occur* — **a suite passing under every value of a variable is blind to it, not robust to it**, and the unvaried variable was the login role.
- **⚠ The extraction alone did not close it, and that is the entry's lesson.** QA's inversion — reverting both fixtures to `except Exception: pytest.skip(...)` — **still passed 14/14** after the predicate was extracted and thoroughly tested, because nothing asserted the fixtures **called** it. Testing the helper moves the untested boundary up a level. It took **three** iterations: helper-tested (still green), fixture-body-tested via `__wrapped__` (**still green — 26 passed, 2 skipped**), and only then RED.
- **⚠⚠ The third layer generalises past this repo.** `pytest.skip()` raises `Skipped`, a **BaseException that `pytest.raises(SomeError)` does not catch** — so it escapes the test and pytest marks **the test itself SKIPPED, not failed**. A test written as `with pytest.raises(X): fixture()` against a fixture that improperly skips reports **skipped, and the suite stays green**. *The test guarding against improper skipping was itself silently skippable, by the very mechanism under test.* The general rule, per QA: **any test whose body can reach a skip sink loses its ENTIRE assertion set, silently.** pgTAP is structurally immune because `select plan(N)` turns an unrun assertion into a **plan mismatch — a failure**; pytest has no plan count.
- **A second, UNGOVERNED skip policy was found and reconciled** — `test_dbase_setup._construct_backend_or_skip` skipped on `ValueError`, the exact case the fixture policy makes FAIL. So `main` briefly held **two skip policies that disagreed about whether a missing credential is a defect**, with the ungoverned one covering S13's acceptance test. Written *in reaction to* the over-broad `except`, it reproduced a milder form of it. Reconciled as **one policy, two call sites** — two copies of one policy drift.
- **The scope question is NOT discharged and is not this entry's** — which incumbent tables V1 adopts remains **PM + Architect's**, tracked independently. **7 of 10 `PFinBackend` ingest paths still target tables no V1 migration creates.**
- **Source.** Observed 2026-08-09 immediately after **S13** (PR #357). With construction no longer raising, `test_table_reflection` and most of `test_dbase_update.py` fail. **None of it is caused by S13** — it was invisible only because `PFinBackend()` raised first.
- **What it is.** The already-documented incumbent-vs-V1 schema drift recorded in `workers/CLAUDE.md`: `pfin.cpi` / `equity_profile` / `reporting_period` / the three statement tables / `earning` / `asset_cat` are created by **no V1 migration**. The ETL still expects the incumbent's shape.
- **⚠ Filed as a note, not a new defect, deliberately.** The V1-scope question — which incumbent tables V1 adopts, and which are dropped — is **PM + Architect's and already tracked**. Opening a second home for it here is how two answers diverge. **This entry exists only to record that the failures are now VISIBLE**, so the next person to run the integration lane does not mistake a pre-existing scope gap for a regression introduced by S13.
- **⚠ Related posture weakness, worth its own attention.** `tests/conftest.py`'s `backend` fixture wraps construction in `except Exception: pytest.skip(...)`, which converts a **production-fatal** failure into a **green skip**. Measured during S13: in the pre-fix RED run, the pre-existing fixture-based tests **SKIPPED on the very ArgumentError that blocks the production entry point**, while purpose-built tests failed. That fixture is a fourth layer of concealment on top of S11/S12/S13, and it is why "the ETL tests are green" carried no information about whether the ETL could start. Backend correctly did **not** modify it (other tests depend on it). **Narrowing that `except` is the actionable half of this entry.**
- **Owner.** PM + Architect (the scope question) · Backend (the fixture's `except` breadth). **Not blocking.**
- **⚠ Amendment 2026-08-09 — narrowing the `except` is necessary and NOT sufficient, and the reason generalises.** The fixture's breadth is only half the concealment; the other half is that **every run of that lane authenticates as `postgres`**, a superuser. Narrowing the exception makes a failure *louder*; it does not make a role-privilege failure *occur*. **A suite that passes under every value of a variable is not robust to that variable — it is blind to it**, and the variable here is the login role, not the exception class. So the actionable half is properly *narrow the `except` **and** run the lane under a non-superuser login*; narrowing alone would have left **S17** (below) exactly as invisible as it was. Recorded because the first framing of this entry named the `except` alone.

**S17. ~~`SBaseConn` never assumes a database role, so every statement it issues fails `42501` under the production login.~~ — ✅ DISCHARGED 2026-08-09 (PRs #372 + #377), PROVEN under a real `pfin_etl` login.** [was: ⚠ PRODUCTION-FATAL for every ETL table path]
- **✅ What shipped.** A `_role(session, role)` context manager assumed **explicitly at each call site**, transaction-locally: **role argument required with no default** (a default makes the privileged choice invisible at the call site), an **allowlist `{authenticated, service_role}`** that doubles as the injection fence (`SET ROLE` takes an identifier and cannot be parameterised), and `impersonate()`'s **N1 teardown shape** so a failed `reset role` on an aborted transaction cannot replace the original exception. **Reads `authenticated`, writes `service_role`** per the ADR-023 amendment. **No migration, no grant** — granting `pfin_etl` anything directly would invert `055`.
- **✅ PROVEN IN BOTH DIRECTIONS, by an operator, under a real LOGIN — not by a fixture.** **RED:** `permission denied for schema pfin` (SQLSTATE **42501**), then `count = 0` after `set role service_role` — *same session, same credential*, which is what makes it a privilege finding rather than a broken table. **GREEN:** `pfin.cpi_u_index` **0 → 137 rows**, zero nulls. **Idempotence:** a second run held the count flat at 137 with `ingested_at` **re-stamped**, proving it executed the `ON CONFLICT` path rather than no-opping.
- **⚠⚠ THE FIX AS FIRST MERGED (#372) WAS INCOMPLETE, AND ONLY THE MANUAL RUN FOUND IT.** `_role()` wrapped every **data** method; `metadata.reflect()` lives in `_sbase_setup`, called from `__init__`, **ahead of every data path** — so `PFinBackend()` died at construction and no data-layer test could observe it, because none of them got that far. **Every automated check passed on the unfixed code.** Fixed at #377 by assuming the read role for reflection. **A fix covering every statement except the first one covers nothing.**
- **The fault is schema `USAGE`, one gate EARLIER than the table privileges this entry was filed against** — `has_schema_privilege('pfin_etl','pfin','USAGE')` → **`f`** — which is why the operator's RED named the **schema** rather than a table.
- **⚠ A prior ruling was measured FALSE here:** that `_sbase_setup` reflects from world-readable `pg_catalog`, so construction succeeds privilege-less and the fault always surfaces at first DML. It does not — the dialect's reflection query filters on the schema and Postgres requires `USAGE` to inspect it. **Construction is the fault site.**
- **`update_table_df` was REPAIRED, not merely hardened** — see the `(a′)` finding: staging was seeded `AS SELECT * FROM target`, so every updated key matched **two** staging rows and the `UPDATE … FROM` join, documented as unpredictable, **took the stale one**. Measured: `UPDATE 3` where 2 were intended, both updates **silently discarded**, every untouched row self-updated. **The FMP price-refresh path did not reliably update anything.** Latent, not an incident: `pfin.eod_price` held **zero rows** and is the only live V1 caller.
- **⚠ The standing lesson, and the reason this entry is long.** This was the **seventh** concealment layer in one stack, and each was invisible to the layer above it — this one invisible to *the fix for the layer below it*. **The only thing that has reliably found them is a human running the assembled path.** That is the argument **S10** was filed to make, now demonstrated twice.
- **Source.** Found 2026-08-09 by Backend while planning the CPI-U backfill after **S13** was merged. Established statically from the tree at `832cfb7` — no run required, and none of it was visible to any green signal we hold.
- **Mechanism, measured.**
  - `053:185–186` grants `select` on `pfin.cpi_u_index` to `authenticated` and `select, insert, update` to `service_role`. **Nothing is granted to `pfin_etl`.**
  - `055` **deliberately** grants `pfin_etl` no direct table privileges — *"the `service_role` grant architecture remains the SINGLE place table privileges are decided"* — and creates it **NOINHERIT**. A NOINHERIT session therefore holds **no privileges at all** until an explicit `SET [LOCAL] ROLE`.
  - `_SET_WRITE_ROLE = "set local role service_role"` occurs in **`nav_daily.py` only** (grep across `workers/etl/src`). `connection.py`'s sole `set local role` is `authenticated`, inside `impersonate()`. **`TenantBoundConnection.system()` sets no role at all.**
  - ⇒ every `SBaseConn` statement — `fetch_table_df`, `_fetch_sbase_ldict`, `insert_table_df`, `update_table_df`, `upsert_table_df`, `_staging_update` — executes **privilege-less** and fails `42501`.
- **⚠ It is not "the write path" — it is every statement.** `authenticated` and `service_role` both hold SELECT; NOINHERIT `pfin_etl` holds neither ambiently, so **reads fail identically to writes**. `update_table_asset` reads before it writes and dies on the read.
- **Why it stayed invisible — two reasons, and the second is the one worth keeping.** **(i)** Every local run to date authenticates as **`postgres`**, a superuser, which needs no role assumption — so the defect is *invariant under every run anyone has performed*, and no amount of test-hardening in that configuration could surface it. **(ii)** **S10** exercised `nav_daily` — **the one worker that does assume the role, and the only one.** Precisely S13's shape: the path that got run was the path that bypasses the defect. **That is now three times in this section** (S13 hidden behind S11's barrier; S13 hidden from S10 by worker choice; S17 hidden from S10 by the same worker choice) — the recurring generator is *verifying through whichever path is easiest to reach*, and the easiest path is systematically the least representative one.
- **⚠ THE TRAP, stated plainly because it is the thing most likely to be got wrong next.** A local backfill run as `postgres` **will succeed and will populate `pfin.cpi_u_index`.** **Rows in that table are not evidence that the importer works.** Whoever next sees a non-zero row count must ask **which role wrote them** before reading it as discharge — a superuser-written table is indistinguishable, by inspection of the table, from a correctly-written one.
- **Blast radius.** The same surface S13 blocked — the whole `PFinBackend` API, `main.py:48`'s `update_table_all()`, the CPI-U import, the FMP ingest — but at a **different layer**, and **it does not reproduce locally under a superuser**. Deploy-fatal for the ETL container: it fails on the first scheduled run, after construction succeeds.
- **AC.** Assume the role **explicitly and transaction-locally at the call sites**, mirroring `nav_daily`'s `_SET_WRITE_ROLE`. ⚠ **Do NOT centralise it as an engine-level connect/execute listener** — that confers **ambient privilege for the engine's lifetime** and defeats the entire purpose of `055`'s NOINHERIT choice; it is the tempting option and it is the wrong one. **RED first:** the acceptance run must be shown failing `42501` on `main`, under a **real `pfin_etl` LOGIN**, before the fix — otherwise the fix is unfalsifiable and we have no more evidence than we started with. ~~**One measured unknown to settle in the same pass:** whether `service_role` holds **TEMP** on the database, since `_staging_update` creates a temp staging table.~~ **✅ MEASURED 2026-08-09 — `has_database_privilege('service_role', current_database(), 'TEMP')` → `t`.** Also `t` for `pfin_etl`: **TEMP comes from `PUBLIC` at the database level, not from any grant**, which is why `055` granting nothing does not take it away. **No grants migration; nothing for Architect.** ⚠ Bounded to the **local** stack, on `055`'s own principle that local measurements do not transfer — if a production cluster revokes TEMP from `PUBLIC` this flips, so it belongs in the deploy checks rather than being retired as settled.
- **✅ AC ADDENDUM — the entry-point bound. This statement is REQUIRED in PR-2's body; it is not optional colour.** *(Sec's finding — raised at the D-A ruling; framing sharpened by Backend and ruled to carry that form. Recorded with both attributions rather than collapsing either.)*
  > **S17 discharged for the helper layer.** A green here demonstrates that the `SBaseConn` helpers can authenticate and write against **the three tables that exist** — it does **not** demonstrate the ETL entry point runs. It cannot: `main.py` / `mini.py` call `update_table_all()`, which dies at `pfin.cpi` before reaching anything the fix touches. **The entry point remains blocked on the V1-scope question routed to PM + Architect — 7 of 10 ingest paths target tables no V1 migration creates.**
  - **⚠ The bound is not merely that the discharge is partial.** It is that **the S17 fix is unfalsifiable at the entry-point level until the V1-scope question resolves** — nobody can demonstrate the ETL container works, **in either direction** — and **that state persists silently, because a green helper-layer test looks like success.** That is the **S16** lesson at the right altitude: the hazard is not the missing coverage, it is that **the green signal invites the broader inference**. Whoever reads "S17 fixed" must not thereby read "the ETL works".
  - **Booked here rather than held for PR-2 deliberately.** PR-2 is gated on an F/CTO go **and** an ADR-023 amendment ratify — an unbounded wait across an unknown number of session boundaries. §7.7's own ruling governs: ***"Booked rather than held in conversation because 'wait' is the disposition that gets lost."***
- **✅ D-A RULED (Sec, 2026-08-09) — recorded so it is not re-derived at build time.** Option **(c)** approved: a `_role()` context manager, explicit at each call site. **(b) — an engine-level listener — is VETOED on two independent grounds:** it confers ambient privilege for the engine's lifetime, **and** it would alter `TenantBoundConnection`'s semantics, which [ADR-011](DECISIONS.md#adr-011) Decision 4 names **by name** as the code-layer fence. Three requirements on `_role()`: **no default role argument** (a default makes the privileged choice invisible at the call site — (b)'s defect in a different costume); **allowlist `{service_role, authenticated}`**, which doubles as the injection fence since `SET ROLE` cannot be parameterised; and **reuse `impersonate()`'s N1 teardown shape**, or an `InFailedSqlTransaction` on teardown replaces the original exception exactly where diagnosis matters.
- **⚠ Backend's `service_role`-for-reads lean was VETOED, and the reason supersedes the *"these tables carry no tenant, which argues `service_role` for both"* premise this entry previously carried (deleted in this commit).** **The premise is false for TWO of the three tables that exist**, not one:
  - **`pfin.asset`** — `016` declares it **HYBRID**: `users_id` NULLABLE, `asset_select` = `using (users_id is null or users_id = auth.uid())`, so non-NULL rows are a user's private assets (house, custom holdings).
  - **`pfin.eod_price`** — `019`'s `eod_price_select` is **asset-anchored** (`exists (select 1 from pfin.asset a where a.asset_id = eod_price.asset_id and (a.users_id is null or a.users_id = auth.uid()))`) and the table carries **per-user `manual_valuation` rows**, with INSERT / UPDATE / DELETE each gated on `source = 'manual_valuation'` **and** `a.users_id = auth.uid()`. Under `service_role`, `select * from pfin.eod_price` returns **every tenant's manually-entered valuations — user-entered money figures — overwritable by the same mechanism.**
  - ⚠ **Booked as a fix, not a note, for a specific reason:** a reader implementing from this ledger would conclude only `asset` needs `authenticated` reads and **reopen the veto on the table holding user-entered financial values.**
- **The hazard in one line.** Under `service_role` the read pulls **every tenant's private rows** into worker memory, **and the read is the smaller half**: `update_table_df` bulk-UPDATEs from that frame, so an FMP profile refresh could silently overwrite another tenant's private row. **Widening the read role to fix a permissions defect would have manufactured a cross-tenant WRITE hazard** — fixing a fail-closed bug by failing open, one layer down. **RULING: reads `authenticated`, writes `service_role`.** `authenticated` with no JWT is not degraded — it resolves to exactly the global partition wanted, fail-closed, with no SQL change.
- **Grounding, so this does not read as an empirical finding about two tables' shapes.** [ADR-011](DECISIONS.md#adr-011) Decision 1 clause (b) says ***"Writes* execute under `service_role`"** and is **silent on reads**. Reading it as *"the privileged context runs as `service_role`"* is **the widening that produced the hazard** — the table shapes are where it surfaced, not why it was wrong. *(Carried canonically by the ADR-023 amendment; the redundancy here is deliberate.)*
- **✅ Two BLOCKING test controls (Sec), beyond the legs already specified.** **(i)** Assert SQLSTATE **`42501` by code** — not by message text, not by "it raised" — on **both** the read and write legs, against tables that exist. ⚠ **Scoped to `fetch_table_df` / `upsert_table_df` against real tables, and NOT to "any statement" — do not narrow or tidy this without re-deriving why.** TEMP comes from **`PUBLIC` at the database level** and is granted by no migration, so **TEMP DDL succeeds privilege-less**. A proof statement that happens to be TEMP DDL **will not observe `42501`** — it will succeed, and **a RED that never appears reads as a fix that already works.** **(ii)** A **per-table read-set parity check**, with the criterion stated so it does not have to be re-derived from the rationale: **for each table the fix touches, in the same run — `count(*) as service_role` − `count(*) as authenticated` **==** `count(*) matching that table's user-partition predicate`** (**0** for a pure-global table).
  - ⚠ **The predicate is PER-TABLE and is not always `users_id is not null` — MEASURED, and the two fixes above do not compose without this.** `pfin.eod_price` **has no `users_id` column at all** (verified live: only `asset` carries one; `eod_price` and `cpi_u_index` do not). It is **asset-anchored**, so its partition predicate is `exists (select 1 from pfin.asset a where a.asset_id = eod_price.asset_id and a.users_id is not null)`. **A criterion written as a bare `users_id is not null` is INEXPRESSIBLE on `eod_price`** — the very table Drift 1 added to this veto for holding user-entered money. Instantiations: `asset` → `users_id is not null`; `eod_price` → the asset-anchored EXISTS above; `cpi_u_index` → pure-global, expected delta **0**.
  - **📐 HOW TO INSTANTIATE IT — the mechanical rule, which is the reusable part.** ***Take the table's SELECT policy's `USING` clause, set `auth.uid()` to NULL, and negate what remains.*** Worked on `eod_price`: `eod_price_select` is `exists (… a.users_id is null **or** a.users_id = auth.uid())`; with `auth.uid()` NULL the visible set collapses to `exists (… a.users_id is null)`; the delta is its **complement**, `exists (… a.users_id is not null)`. **Derived from the policy, not intuited from the table.** ⚠ **Stated because *"write that table's user-partition predicate"* on its own re-opens the exact authoring risk that produced both of this section's compose-failures** — an author instantiating from their mental model of the table. The rule above is **checkable by a reviewer**; "write the tenant predicate" is not.
  - **🎯 The property the criterion serves**, so a new table's instantiation can be sanity-checked against intent rather than only pattern-matched: ***the `authenticated` read must be a SUPERSET of the rows the write path can collide with.*** That is what makes the read-then-diff-then-insert loop safe; **the count comparison is a proxy for it**, not the thing itself. If an instantiation satisfies the counts but breaks the superset property, the instantiation is wrong.
  - 📌 **Provenance, stated rather than absorbed: the generalization from `users_id is not null` to *"that table's user-partition predicate"* is BACKEND's, not Sec's, and rides Sec's in-flight fidelity check for confirmation.** Sec's criterion was authored against `asset`'s shape and is correct there; it does not reach `eod_price`, which Sec added in the same pass. Recorded this way rather than silently corrected — **editing a ruling to make it work is how a ledger stops matching the ruling it cites.** The hazard it catches: a table with no `authenticated` SELECT policy returns **zero rows silently**, the diff reads "all new", and the ETL **mass-inserts**. ⚠ **That failure mode is created by the role split itself**, so it did not exist before this fix. A table failing that check must **fail the test** — it must **not** be worked around by widening the role. **No cross-tenant fixture required** (Sec named this explicitly so one is not added defensively).
- **⚠ Conditional that re-opens Sec's veto surface:** if the fix acquires **any `grant`** — especially a direct table privilege to `pfin_etl` — that **inverts `055`'s posture** and returns to Sec as a fresh veto. PR-2's body must state **"no migration authored"**. Also corrected against `053`: the grant is `select, insert, update` to `service_role`, and **the `select` is load-bearing for the `ON CONFLICT` arbiter read** — do not tighten it.
- **Owner.** Backend (the code) + **Sec joint-review as a MERGE GATE**, not optional review — [ADR-011](DECISIONS.md#adr-011) Decision 1 (privileged-context-write): this decides *where privilege is assumed*. ~~Architect **conditionally** (a grants migration only if the TEMP measurement comes back short).~~ **Architect's conditional is DISCHARGED — TEMP measured `t`; there is no grants migration to author.** ⚠ **A new prerequisite replaced it: PR-2 is additionally gated on an [ADR-023](DECISIONS.md#adr-023) amendment (read role-of-record + the partition-class rule), F/CTO-ratified BEFORE the build** — the read-role split is standing policy for **every future hybrid table** and is not derivable from either existing ADR, so it cannot ride the implementation PR. **Deploy blocker for the ETL container.** The fix itself authors **no migration**, changes **no RLS policy**, moves **no §10 catalogued instance** (RT-22 / RT-26 / RT-27 unaffected) and expands **no Decision 3 family** instance.

**S18. ~~The CPI-U backfill has no committed entry point — the only ways to invoke it are a test or a scratchpad.~~ — ✅ DISCHARGED 2026-08-09 (PR #372).** [was: gap — reproducibility; an S10 anti-pattern]
- **✅ What shipped.** `workers/etl/run_cpi_backfill.py`, mirroring `run_nav_daily.py`, carrying **why it exists** and **why `main.py` cannot reach it** (`update_table_all()` dies at `pfin.cpi` first). A non-integer year argument **raises rather than defaulting**, because a typo silently becoming a full backfill would report success for a run that asked for something else.
- **It did the job it was filed for.** The S17 acceptance run was executed **through this committed runner**, by the operator — which is the property that distinguishes a discharge from an anecdote: **reproducible by someone other than its author.**
- **Source.** Found 2026-08-09 by Backend while planning the backfill run. **Grep-verified at `832cfb7`:** `backfill_cpi_u_index` has **zero callers** outside `workers/etl/tests/test_dbase_update.py:51`.
- **Mechanism.** `run_nav_daily.py` is the committed runner for the W-1 worker. **There is no `run_cpi_backfill.py`.** `main.py` and `mini.py` both call `update_table_all()`, which is a different path and never reaches the backfill.
- **⚠ Why this is more than a convenience gap.** The backfill is **by design a one-shot invoked on demand**, so the only recorded ways to run it are **(i)** pytest through the `backend` fixture — whose over-broad `except` is S16's actionable half — or **(ii)** an uncommitted scratchpad. **(ii) is exactly the shape S10 named:** *a verification gate that cannot be run without writing or patching code outside the repo tends not to get run.* **S11 is this section's own worked example of what that costs** — a barrier nobody had to name kept the assembled path unrun, and the defect behind it sat latent for ~50 migrations. **Committing the runner is what makes S17's acceptance run reproducible by someone other than its author**, which is the property that distinguishes a discharge from an anecdote.
- **⚠ Related ordering consequence, recorded here because it is what makes a dedicated runner necessary rather than merely tidy.** `update_table_all()` calls `update_table_cpi()` — targeting `pfin.cpi`, **created by no V1 migration** — **before** `update_table_cpi_u_index()`. So the container entry point raises `UndefinedTable` and **cannot reach CPI-U even after S17 is fixed**. The scope question itself belongs to **S16** and PM + Architect; only the sequencing consequence is recorded here.
- **AC.** Commit `run_cpi_backfill.py` mirroring `run_nav_daily.py`'s shape. Rides the S17 PR — the two are one deliverable in practice, since S17's evidence depends on S18's runner existing.
- **Owner.** Backend. **Not blocking on its own; it blocks any reproducible discharge of S17.**

**S19. The ETL loader accepted only the PRODUCTION BLS name, so a developer's `.env` had to hold a production-named credential.** [convention gap — auditability, not exposure]
- **Source.** Raised by Sec at the S12 review, 2026-08-09; landed with S12 (PR #369) because it rides the same `load_api_keys()` split. **Number allocated at insert (next-free after S18), not carried from the proposal** — an ID baked into a committed doc before allocation is the follow-up-number-that-names-nothing failure.
- **⚠ It is an EXTENSION beyond S12's AC, not part of it, and MUST NOT be closed by S12 passing.** S12 rules **where the raise happens** (point of use, not load). This rules **which name the key carries**. They share a function and nothing else; closing this because S12 is green would retire an unmet requirement.
- **Mechanism.** The secrets manifest's convention rests on **distinct NAMES carrying the tier**. The code defeated that at the local boundary: **`BLS_API_KEY_TEST` had no reader**, and the loader took the production name unprefixed — so a developer machine necessarily held a **production-named** credential whatever value was in it. The manifest's third-tier block states plainly that the non-overlap invariant **does not reach local dev and cannot**; this is the loader half of that boundary.
- **✅ What shipped.** A per-key env-name preference: **`BLS_API_KEY_TEST` preferred when present, falling back to `BLS_API_KEY`**, so deployed containers — which set only the production name — are unaffected. `.env.example` now documents the TEST name as the one a developer sets. A test pins that the override is **per-key and does not generalise**, since it was ruled for BLS specifically.
- **⚠ IT IS NOT A FENCE AND MUST NOT BE DESCRIBED AS ONE.** Nothing can inspect a developer's `.env`, and nothing should try. CI cannot observe a local machine. What it buys is that the production name is **absent from developer machines entirely**, so *"is there a production-named credential on this laptop?"* becomes a question with a **checkable right answer of NO** — the discipline made auditable **by shape rather than by trust**. Anyone extending this should not add a check that pretends otherwise.
- **✅ CODE HALF DISCHARGED 2026-08-09 (PR #369); ⚠ THE PRACTICE HALF IS NOT, AND THE ENTRY STAYS OPEN BECAUSE OF IT.** The loader now prefers `BLS_API_KEY_TEST` and falls back to `BLS_API_KEY`, and `.env.example` documents the TEST name as the one a developer sets. **But the whole point of this entry is that the PRODUCTION NAME IS ABSENT FROM DEVELOPER MACHINES** — and it is not yet: the local `.env` still holds the key under `BLS_API_KEY` (established by checking the variable NAME only, never the value). **Outstanding: rename that one line to `BLS_API_KEY_TEST`**, which the loader already prefers. No code impact.
- **⚠ Deliberately NOT struck.** A strikethrough reads as fully closed, and the code half alone does not deliver the property: *"is there a production-named credential on this laptop?"* still answers **YES**. **Marking it done because the code shipped would be the exact shape of claiming coverage a change does not provide** — this entry's own subject is a convention that holds only if practice follows it.
- **Owner.** Backend (the loader — **done**) + DevOps (the manifest side, PR #367 — done) + Sec (tier scoping) + **F/CTO (the one-line `.env` rename — the outstanding half)**. **Not blocking.**

**S20. ~~A BLS period published with no value was dropped SILENTLY, so a gap in `pfin.cpi_u_index` was undetectable by construction.~~ — ✅ DISCHARGED 2026-08-10 (PR #380).** [was: observability defect — the drop was correct, the silence was not]
- **Source.** Found 2026-08-10 by following up the S17 acceptance run, which populated `cpi_u_index` with **137 rows where a contiguous Jan-2015 → Jun-2026 series is 138**. Number allocated at insert as next-free after S19.
- **⚠ The drop was never the defect.** BLS returns a period it has published nothing for with the literal value `"-"` — **measured against the live API for `2025-M10`, a real non-publication**. A `strict=False` cast turns that into null and `drop_nulls` removes the row. **Storing it is impossible anyway:** `053` carries `NOT NULL` plus a finiteness CHECK on `cpi_u_index.cpi_value`, so **the drop is FORCED BY THE SCHEMA and is correct.**
- **What was wrong is that nothing, at any layer, distinguished *"BLS published no value"* from *"we lost a row"*** — and `cpi_u_index`'s PK is `cpi_period DATE` with **nothing asserting contiguity**, so the gap was undetectable by construction and propagated into inflation-adjusted figures.
- **⚠ The diagnosis pointed at the wrong mechanism, and following the pointer would have missed it.** The drop was expected in `clean_empty_str_df`; that helper is **not on this path at all** (it serves the `pfin.cpi` path). The drop is in `fetch_cpi_df`, **one layer earlier**. *A correct diagnosis naming the wrong mechanism is still wrong in the way that matters* — checking the mechanism rather than the conclusion is what located it.
- **✅ What shipped.** A **per-row warning** naming the period and stating that the series has a **real gap and this is not a fetch failure** — without which the log would trade silence for a **false alarm**, inviting the next reader to "fix" a drop the schema forces. Plus an **unconditional count reconciliation** (*N returned, M kept, K dropped*). **The reconciliation is the load-bearing half:** a per-row warning alone is still silent about a *systematic* drop, and a future change removing rows for a different reason would log nothing. **Emitted even when nothing is dropped** — otherwise its absence would be ambiguous, and *"nothing dropped"* and *"the logging broke"* would look identical, which is the same indistinguishability this closes.
- **The contract question was routed, not resolved here** — whether `cpi_u_index` consumers tolerate gaps belongs to Architect + PM, and **F/CTO ruled Option C: the value table stays strictly non-null, with a separate observation record for published-but-unusable periods.** This entry made the gap visible; the ADR decides what depends on it.
- **Owner.** Backend (the reconciliation — done) · Architect + PM (the consumer contract — ruled). **Not blocking.**

**S21. `fetch_cpi_df` discards value-null periods from its own return value, so `pfin.cpi_u_nonpublication` cannot be populated by any writer — and the fix has TWO HALVES THAT MUST SHIP TOGETHER.** [gap — blocks ADR-049 from doing anything; ⚠ splitting it is a regression]
- **Source.** Found 2026-08-10 during the [ADR-049](DECISIONS.md#adr-049) migration's Sec joint-review (PR #389), as blocking condition C3. Number allocated at insert (next-free after S20), not carried from the proposal.
- **Mechanism, measured at `workers/etl/src/pfin_back_etl/utils.py:513–515`** — inside `fetch_cpi_df`, before the return: `n_before = len(df)` / `dropped = df.filter(value.is_null())` / `df = df.drop_nulls(subset=["value"])`. **The `2025-10` row — the exact subject of `063` — never leaves the transport.** It is logged and discarded.
- **⚠ WHY THIS WAS WORSE THAN AN OMISSION, and the reason it blocked a merge.** `063`'s header initially instructed a future writer to reuse the CPI-U mapper's month projection but not its value filter. That is **correct and unexecutable**: the rows are destroyed one layer earlier, so there is nothing to apply the projection to. A writer following the documented requirement gets an empty record, **has discharged the stated obligation, and has no reason to look further.** An omission invites a question; a false sense of completion answers it in advance.
- **⚠⚠ THE TWO HALVES ARE ONE ITEM. Landing the first alone makes things strictly worse.** **(i)** Lift or opt-out the transport drop so a consumer that wants value-null periods can receive them. **(ii)** Land the **count reconciliation**: *periods returned by transport = rows upserted to `053` + rows recorded in `063`*, **failing loud on imbalance**. Today `063` is **correctly empty** — empty is its only reachable state. The moment (i) ships without (ii), "empty" silently changes meaning from *correct* to *broken*, **and no test, constraint, or layer would notice** — the two worlds are identical at every layer. **Do not schedule these as two tickets.**
- **The enforceable half is already half-built and must not be reinvented.** `fetch_cpi_df` computes returned / kept / dropped, and its own note states *"THE RECONCILIATION IS THE LOAD-BEARING HALF, NOT THE PER-ROW LINE. A per-row warning still says nothing about a SYSTEMATIC drop… Counting in and out catches the class."* One assertion catches both obstacles **without either being individually remembered**, and catches a future drop added for some third reason.
- **⚠ Also required: the raw token.** `cast(pl.Float64, strict=False)` overwrites the column in place, so the literal `'-'` is destroyed before the drop. `063.published_value_raw` is nullable and its comment anticipates this, so the schema is already correct — but the transport cannot currently supply the token either.
- **⚠ The parser fence rule, ruled by Sec 2026-08-10 and easy to get backwards.** A non-publication writer **is** the "second live consumer" that `fetch_cpi_df`'s note names as its fence trigger. But the two consumers **agree on grain and are exactly opposed on value**. So: **grain fence permitted (both want it; optional) · value fence FORBIDDEN (it would destroy this table's subject) · the existing value drop must be LIFTED or made opt-out — which runs opposite to "add a fence."**
- **Test venue.** `workers/etl` pytest, not the DB layer. **No database test can see a process that never ran** — QA stated this as explicit non-coverage rather than leaving it implied.
- **Owner.** Backend (`workers/etl`). **Not blocking any merge; blocking ADR-049 from having any effect.** ⚠ "ADR-049 has landed" must not be read as "the record is working."

**S22. Stalled-ingest detection is assigned to NO OWNER anywhere.** [gap — surfaced by withdrawing a false belief, not by new work]
- **Source.** Found 2026-08-10 in team-lead cross-check of the ADR-049 Sec verdict (PR #389).
- **Mechanism.** `fn_cpi_u_index_for_period`'s trailing-edge bound is data-derived (a period is a gap only if a *later* period exists), which is inside [ADR-049](DECISIONS.md#adr-049) Decision 3's *"or a stricter bound"* grant and consults no clock. Its accepted cost, stated in the migration: **it cannot detect a stalled ingest** — a dead ETL yields `beyond_coverage` forever, indistinguishable from "not yet published."
- **⚠ How the gap was concealed.** The verdict accepting that trade cited ADR-049 D2 as *"already assigning stalled-ingest detection to a run log."* D2's actual words are *"two operational states that **a run log already narrows**"* — a subordinate clause justifying the rejection of option C′, which **assigns nothing.** Sec conceded the overstatement on cross-check. **The ACCEPT survives on other grounds; the assignment never existed.** So the helper's disclaimer — *"ingest-freshness monitoring is NOT this function's job"* — is **accurate and pointing at nobody.**
- **The generalisable shape:** a correct scope-boundary statement reads like a routing statement. *"Not my job"* is only safe when someone else's job says otherwise.
- **AC.** Name an owner and a venue for ETL run-freshness monitoring. Do **not** solve it inside the helper — the boundary is right. **This entry must not close as a comment in a migration.**
- **Owner.** Backend + DevOps (venue TBD). Not blocking.

**S23. Two defects in `053_cpi_u_index.sql`, both confirmed by direct inspection, both needing a vehicle it cannot supply itself.** [refinement + latent defect]
- **Source.** Found 2026-08-10 during ADR-049 migration authoring; Sec confirmed both and ruled them a **separate vehicle** that does not block PR #389.
- **(a) The `source` column comment is FALSE as written.** It claims the column exists so a future series *"can coexist without a schema change."* `053`'s PK is **`cpi_period` alone**, so two series would collide on period — they cannot coexist. ⚠ Being a `comment on`, it **has a database representation**, so per the `apply-migration` rule it can only be corrected by a **new comment-only migration** (the `052` shape), never by editing the merged file.
- **(b) `053` has no first-of-month CHECK**, though its comment says the period is normalized to first-of-month. Only `cpi_u_index_value_finite` exists. `063` enforces one; `053` does not.
