### §7.11 — Open findings from the 2026-08-11 verification-discipline session

*Filed 2026-08-11. **These are RECORDED, not worked** — the session that produced them consumed itself on process at the expense of product, and the correct response was to write them down and stop. Each entry states the finding and its routing; none is investigated further here. **The heading carries no count**, per the ruling that a count in a §7 heading goes stale on the first append.*

*⚠ **The "deliberately not duplicated here" carve-out is VOID — the items it excluded are in §7.12 below.** It read: the `app_id` blind spot, `053`'s first-of-month grain hole, the sweep buckets, the rejected `marker_basis` option, S22, `test_staging_update.py`'s unqualified `drop table` and `db-tests.yml` never going red live in `MILESTONES.md` "Pending", and a second home would drift. **`## Pending` was removed on 2026-08-12** — no purpose, no size bound, no owner, ~90% of an auto-load that was being truncated to nothing. There is no first home left for them to drift from.*

**`067` — the catalog-comment completion.** [pointer]
- **Source.** Sec's concession during the `066` review, after initially blocking it. `066`'s **catalog** comment names the GRANT and the ACL and is **silent on the COMMENT**, though the DROP takes both — the same omission corrected in `066`'s header, surviving in the copy a `\d+` reader with no repo actually sees.
- **Disposition.** A **dedicated** `052`-shape comment-only migration. **Ride-along was considered and rejected**: the trigger for a ride-along is plausibly the very migration the sentence warns, so waiting gates the fix on the failure it prevents.
- **Tracked in Linear.** Recorded here only so it is visible from the repo. Owner: **Architect**. Routing: **Sec** (catalog surface on a function feeding financial figures).

**§10 catalogued instances — does a catalogued instance require an OPERATING assertion, or is an enforcement MECHANISM sufficient?** [ruling needed]
- **Source.** Architect, from the six-week dead-hook finding. Academic **today** — every catalogued fence lives in GitHub Actions, which self-reports by construction, goes red per PR, and gates on branch protection. **But that is a property of where those fences happen to live, not of anything [ADR-011](DECISIONS.md#adr-011) Decision 4 requires.** Nothing stops a future catalogued instance being attributed to a layer that cannot report it operates — at which point the layer attribution is an unverified claim, and §10's defense-in-depth argument rests on layers being real.
- **Routing.** **Sec joint-review-mandatory; F/CTO ruling needed.** Recorded, deliberately **not answered** — it is a Decision 4 scope question and answering it in a backlog entry would be the wrong surface.

**`guard-logic-matrix` — a CI watcher for the shared-path commit guard.** [needs F/CTO ratify]
- **Source.** DevOps, ruled **for**: the harness is hermetic and deterministic, and fails **2/6** against a guard-less `pre-commit`, which is what makes a green run mean anything. Cost: one required context (**17 → 18**).
- **⚠ THE NAMING CONDITION IS THE LOAD-BEARING PART, and it must survive into whatever ships.** The job proves the guard's **LOGIC** is intact. It does **NOT** prove the guard is **ARMED** anywhere — armed-ness is untestable in CI. A check named or read as *"the guard is protecting us"* would **recreate the green-while-dead failure one level up**, which is the exact defect the harness exists to answer.
- Owner: **DevOps**. Required-context changes are F/CTO's ratify.

**`WORKFLOW.md`'s self-declared version has stopped tracking `CHANGELOG.md`.** [refinement]
- **Source.** Architect, found while landing the verification rules. `WORKFLOW.md:4` reads `Current version: v1.44`; `CHANGELOG.md`'s newest entry is `v1.191`.
- **The finding is not the gap, it is the silence.** Either the header is stale by a wide margin or the convention was retired — **and nothing in the repo says which.** **A version field nobody watches is the same shape as a fence nobody armed**: a self-description that stopped tracking reality while still reading as authoritative.
- **Not fixed at discovery, deliberately** — the correct next number lives in `CHANGELOG.md`, and asserting one would have invented a version the series passed long ago. Owner: **team-lead** (CoS-owned surface).

**Five artifacts describe the pre-commit hook set at five different accuracies.** [refinement]
- **Source.** Architect + DevOps, from the dead-hook finding. As merged, **only `ruff` and `hadolint` are wired**; `svelte-check` and `eslint` appear in `.husky/pre-commit` **only inside comments** deferring them until SvelteKit scaffolds, and lint/test/type-check are absent.
- **The spread:** [`MILESTONES.md`](MILESTONES.md) is exact (*"ruff + hadolint; JS deferred to Phase 6"*). [`WORKFLOW.md`](WORKFLOW.md) lists the **intended** set and is silent on what is live. `BACKLOG.md`, [`CHANGELOG.md`](CHANGELOG.md) and **`.claude/agents/devops.md`** all describe a lint/test/type-check/svelte-check set that does not exist.
- **⚠ The role-definition instance matters most: it regenerates the error through the agent that owns the surface.** A wrong description in a doc misleads a reader once; a wrong description in an agent definition is re-asserted every time that agent works.
- **Adjacent, not duplicate:** §7.6 S4's Dependencies line already carries this correction *scoped to S4's own sizing*. This entry is the artifact-spread; S4's is the dependency. Owner: **team-lead** to route (spans DevOps + doc surfaces).

**Two authoring rules produced by this session that have no home yet.** [refinement]
- **(i) A citation must state the RELATION it asserts — contains / governs / is-an-instance-of / is-adjacent-to — not merely the target's name.** The generalisable result of the ADR-011 Decision 1 sweep: *"ADR-011 Decision 1"* attached to a `security invoker` read path and survived review precisely because a bare citation asserts nothing checkable. A named relation is falsifiable; a bare name is not.
- **(ii) The no-count discipline needs its boundary stated, because the rule as written invites the error in both directions.** A count over a **growing** list is a maintenance obligation and gets dropped. A count over a **closed, bounded historical measurement** carries no obligation and is **load-bearing evidence** — *"14 sample files were counted as 14 active hooks"* is a **plausible** number, which is why the wrong answer was believed; stripping it removes the part that explains the failure. **Two agents over-applied the discipline in opposite directions on the same day**, which is the argument that the boundary belongs in the rule rather than in the reader.
- Owner: **team-lead** to route to a home (candidates: `brief-drift-catch`, `WORKFLOW.md` Verification discipline). **Recorded here so they are not lost between skills.**

### §7.12 — Carried out of `MILESTONES.md` `## Pending` at its removal

*Filed 2026-08-12, when `## Pending (immediate)` was removed. **These are RECORDED, not worked.** Every entry below existed in that section; nothing here is new analysis. Session state (`main` sha, PR counts, worktree lists) and completed-work narrative were deleted rather than moved — GitHub holds the PR record, and recorded session state guarantees staleness. **Standing constraints did not come here: they went to the file whose reader would get it wrong**, and the ones whose destination was out of this PR's bounds are listed under "relocation pending" at the end. **The heading carries no count.***

**Architect — the `067` revert gap.** [defect]
- `066`'s DROP already destroyed the function comment `065` set. A revert of `066` — a `067` recreating the six-column function — **must re-issue that comment itself**; it cannot lean on `065`, which is applied and will not re-run. **Ship the revert as written and the function lands with no comment at all.**
- Joins the `067` catalog-comment work already recorded in §7.11.

**Architect + Sec — `063`'s catalog comments were never re-checked.** [verification gap]
- `063:447/562/570/603/611` cite ADR-049 Decision 1. **Believed correct but assumed, not verified** — consciously set aside when #427 was scoped to headers. These have a **database representation**, so any wrong ground is a **`052`-shape comment-only migration, not a header edit**.

**Sec — the `WQ>` marker set is verified CORRECT but never verified COMPLETE.** [verification gap]
- The seven marked quotations are accurate. **Nobody checked `066` for withdrawn text that should be marked and is not** — the false-negative direction, unexamined in the session's own sign-off.

**Architect + Backend — `053` does not enforce first-of-month grain; `063` does.** [defect · needs a ticket]
- One malformed `053` row (e.g. `2025-10-15`) changes another period's **value**, **classification**, **`period_was_due`** and **`coverage_through`** for every caller — four outputs from one bad row. `066`'s CONTRACT makes correctness conditional on that grain. **Unowned and not in §7.6.**

**DevOps — `workers/etl/.venv` is not gitignored.** [defect · ordering constraint]
- `git check-ignore` returns nothing for it; root `node_modules/` is ignored, `.venv` is not, so any `uv run` there recreates it untracked.
- ⚠ **ORDERING: add the ignore BEFORE building §7.6 S4's untracked-file pre-push fence**, or the fence's first real firing is a false positive.

**Backend — the ETL suite cannot collect in a fresh environment.** [defect]
- `uv run pytest` dies at `import polars`. Pre-existing. **This is why #424's E402 fix was verified by property rather than end-to-end** — recorded so that verification's bound stays visible.

**DevOps — the sweep buckets will rot into decoration.** [refinement]

**⚠ The `063`-applied contradiction — REPO-SETTLED, DB-UNVERIFIED, and the surviving claim is auto-loaded.** [defect · verification gap]
- **Source.** Two blocks of the removed `## Pending` disagreed. One asserted `063` was **never applied** and the local DB's max migration was `062`; the other asserted `063`–`066` applied, with a row in `pfin.cpi_u_nonpublication`, proven under a real `pfin_etl` login.
- **⚠ A deletion is a decision even when the reasoning was neutral.** #437's body says the contradiction was flagged rather than adjudicated — **that is true of the reasoning and false of the outcome.** The never-applied block was routed DELETE as stale session narrative; the other side's claim survives as the words *"the substrate is complete and measured"* in `MILESTONES.md` `## Active Feature` — which is **above the autoload cutoff**, so it is injected into every session, main and subagent alike.
- **Repo half, settled** (measured 2026-08-12 in the working tree): `supabase/migrations/` holds `063`–`066`, max `066`, 66 files. **The "local max is `062`" claim is false about the repo.** The `v1.190` `## Recent activity` entry — a dated record — independently states the chain was applied and measured under a real login.
- **DB half, NOT settled.** Nothing here reaches `psql`. **Do not assert it in either direction.**
- **What closes it.** Under the real login: `select max(version) from supabase_migrations.schema_migrations;` and `select count(*) from pfin.cpi_u_nonpublication;` — Backend's to run, not Architect's.
- **Why it is worth five minutes.** The cost is asymmetric. If applied, nothing happens. If not, the **auto-loaded** head tells every future session the substrate is ready — and **SELF-218, the named next deliverable, is the first consumer of exactly that substrate.**

**⚠ `AUTOLOAD-CUTOFF` detects DELETION and is blind to DISPLACEMENT — a fence whose stated bound is wrong.** [defect]
- **Source.** Architect, on its own work in #436/#437. The hook warns loudly when **both** anchors are gone — the marker and the `## Pending` heading. That covers deletion, which is the failure that had just happened.
- **The failure it cannot see is the likelier one.** Insert a section above the marker and the head silently grows; move the marker up, or add a section between `## Recent activity` and it, and **the head silently shrinks.** Both anchors are present, the grep finds one, no warning fires — **and a truncated head is byte-indistinguishable from a correct one at the receiving end.**
- **⚠ Why this ranks above a missing fence: it reads as covered.** The marker's own text says *"Moving this line changes what every session sees"*, which is a request for care — and this repo's standing rule is that **a guard depending on care is decoration.** A guard was written against the failure just seen rather than the failure most likely next.
- **Checkable fix shape** (not implemented, deliberately out of the dispatched scope): have the hook assert the emission contains its expected `## ` headings and warn when one is absent. That is decidable inside the hook and fires on displacement in **either** direction.

**Three `## Pending` blocks were deleted on a container-level "already recorded" check.** [verification gap]
- **Source.** Architect, on its own routing in #437. The 13-PR Phase 3 streak summary, the four memory-codification batches, and the two WHAT-LANDED blocks were deleted as redundant. **What was verified was that the containers exist** — `CHANGELOG.md` carries `v1.37`–`v1.48`, `v1.187`, `v1.189`; `MEMORY.md` indexes the memories. **No entry was opened to confirm it holds the content the block held.**
- ⚠ **The routing file said "spot-check two entries before deleting". That spot-check never happened before the delete landed** — the instruction was written and then not executed by its own author.
- **What settles it.** Open `CHANGELOG.md` `v1.37` and `v1.48` and compare against the deleted block, recoverable at `git show 05103fd:MILESTONES.md`. Recoverable content, so this is a cost-to-recover rather than a loss — but **"already recorded" is doing load-bearing work in a merged PR body and is weaker than it reads.**

**§7.11's carve-out note promises §7.12 for an item that went elsewhere.** [defect]
- **Source.** Architect, on its own edit in #437. §7.11's preamble now says the seven previously-excluded items *"are in §7.12 below"*. **Six are. The `app_id` blind spot is not** — its constraint was routed into `.github/required-contexts.tsv`'s expected-posture block, which is the better home, and the sentence promising §7.12 was never adjusted.
- **What settles it.** One sentence in §7.11 naming the manifest as `app_id`'s destination.
- **Why it is worth recording rather than shrugging at:** it is the exact class the removal was fixing — **a pointer that was true when drafted and went false because of a decision made later in the same PR.**

**⚠ "Run hook index 2" is written into two merged PR bodies, and an index is a position, not an identity.** [defect]
- **Source.** Architect. #437 and #438 both instruct the reader to run `.claude/settings.json` hook **index 2** to verify the auto-loaded head. **It was index 1 this morning** and moved when #435 landed a hook ahead of it.
- **The next hook added at the front breaks the instruction silently:** the reader measures the `core.hooksPath` arming hook, which **correctly emits nothing**, and concludes the ledger hook is broken. That is exactly the wrong turn taken on 2026-08-12 — and it is now documented as if it were the fix.

**Three smaller items from the same handoff.** [pointer]

The hook's fallback anchor is `^## Pending`, so it still matches by prefix. **Reformat that heading to start differently and the fallback goes silent** — harmless while the marker stands, which is why two anchors exist, but it is a dependency nobody would guess.

### §7.13 — Chart-of-Accounts / hierarchical-accounts design question (Accounting scope)

*Scope: homed in §7 (staging queue), not §5 — the decision is on the V1 clock via the timing fence below, even though the user-facing Accounting capability it may unlock is V2+ unless the schema-direction answer forces earlier.*

**Chart-of-Accounts + hierarchical-accounts design question (Accounting scope).** [decision item, F/CTO-directed 2026-08-12]
- **Source.** F/CTO 2026-08-12, raised alongside the component-history P-flag but ratified as a SEPARATE, orthogonal question (Option C's ratify makes no CoA commitment; the CoA answer does not reopen Option C's V1/V2 boundary). Product framing (PM): the incumbent sheet workflow implicitly operates a five-element chart — Assets / Liabilities / Equity with equity sub-accounts, e.g. Expenses rolling up under Distributions (E) — over hierarchical groupings, while the product's GL substrate ([ADR-033](DECISIONS.md#adr-033) machinery, §7.3 stream) carries a flat account model + journal engine. User-facing capability a CoA would unlock: statement-shaped reporting (balance-sheet / income-statement style views), the Distributions (E) expenses rollup, and hierarchical account grouping across composition/allocation surfaces.
- **AC.** Architect authors the schema-direction proposal (options + one-way doors) for F/CTO ratify; Sec engagement travels with the proposal (GL/money-flow joint-review surface). Schema-direction framing (Architect, 2026-08-12): three directions, and note the schema has **no ledger-account concept today** to extend — `pfin.account` is a real-world account, `pfin.user_taxonomy` is a per-user classification whose hierarchy is **fixed-depth text columns** (`domain` / `cat` / `sub_cat`) with no parentage, and `pfin.journal` **groups `account_trans` facts** rather than posting to ledger accounts. **(A) Extend the taxonomy spine** — add an accounting element (A/L/E) and true parentage to `user_taxonomy`; cheapest in DDL, but it normalizes a fixed-depth encoding on a table that four FK-shaped columns already reference. **(B) A first-class CoA table alongside it** — conceptually clean, since a ledger account is genuinely neither a real-world account nor a user category, at the cost of a second classification spine every reporting surface must then choose between. **(C) Defer the shape** — nothing in V1 posts to a chart. **One-way doors:** arbitrary-depth parentage means a **self-FK on a per-user table, which joins the [ADR-011](DECISIONS.md#adr-011) Decision 3 cross-tenant FK-bypass family and requires matched-tenant validation in the DDL** (non-negotiable, and expensive to add to a populated table); and re-pointing `user_taxonomy`'s existing referents (`022` / `023` / `029`) at a new spine would be a data migration across already-fenced columns — the costly thing to discover late. **Precedent the proposal must address rather than repeat:** `account.sub_cat_id` was built at `012` and **DROPPED at `048`**, so an account→classification FK has been tried and withdrawn once.
- **Dependencies / timing fence.** Must be answered **no later than the earlier of**: (a) §7.3 GL-substrate milestone sequencing; (b) before any NAV component-checkpoint schema commits to a category key — a coupling **dissolved by [ADR-054](DECISIONS.md#adr-054) Decision 3's per-account-leaf capture** (orthogonality structural per its Decision 6), so fence (b) is vacuous unless that decision is revisited; fence (a) stands on its own clock. Downstream: §5.1 Distributions-presentation deferral; any V2 Accounting-scope capability set.
- **✅ ANSWERED — [ADR-058](DECISIONS.md#adr-058) (taxonomy-vs-GL structural brainstorm 2026-08-18; landed at the GL-split ADR doc-PR).** The schema-direction question is settled by the **asymmetric split**: the posting vocabulary moves out to a new `pfin.posting_prototype`, `pfin.user_taxonomy` keeps its name / its ids / its asset rows and drops `domain`, and **no ledger-account spine is created in V1**. **Entry RETAINED per convention** — it carries the framing the ADR answers, and §5.7's V2+ capability entry points at it. ⚠ **Its one-way-door warning is corrected by DATA, not by argument:** ADR-058 Decision 5 measured the four live FK-shaped referents and found them partitioning cleanly two-and-two along the cut seam, so the asymmetric shape re-targets **two** (`023` / `029`) and leaves `022` / `074` untouched — *"the costly thing to discover late"* was authored without that measurement. **Directions (A) / (B) / (C) as framed above are superseded**: ADR-058's *Alternatives considered* records why a CoA table alongside would have been the **third** classification spine rather than the second, and why hierarchical accounts (`M-hier`) stay gated. Landing bookings this answer produced: **§7.24** (the five items THIS ANSWER produced — §7.24 has since been extended with rename-PR bookings, so do not read the five as the section's size) + §5.3 (GL-native P&L, V2).


### §7.14 — SELF-217 / SELF-218 build-day follow-ups (2026-08-12)

*Recorded per the recorded-unless-runtime-effect rule; cited by title; no counts in this heading. Sources: the two Sec joint-reviews (GREEN verdicts on `4260c18` and `b728ad6`), Architect/QA/Backend session findings. Detail lives in PRs #443 / #445 and the review threads.*

**`053` positivity CHECK on `pfin.cpi_u_index.cpi_value`.** [hardening; Sec-conditioned; joint-review-MANDATORY when it comes]
- **Source.** Sec joint-review of SELF-218/`067` (2026-08-12): `053`'s CHECK is finiteness-only, so a poisoned `0`/negative/NaN/Infinity print reaches the deflator; `067` guards internally (both CPI legs strictly positive else NULL) but the base table holds no fence of its own.
- **AC (Sec's four conditions, binding).** (1) Predicate is **finite AND strictly positive** on the column — `> 0` alone re-admits NaN and Infinity (`NaN > 0` and `Infinity > 0` are both TRUE in Postgres numeric ordering). (2) **ADDITIVE** to `cpi_u_index_value_finite`, which must survive by name — never a replacement. (3) The same PR (or an earlier standalone one — Sec: guard hardening is **no-later-than** the CHECK PR, earlier is better) `create or replace`s `067`'s guard with an explicit NaN clause AND **re-issues its `comment on`** (create-or-replace preserves comments — the DIVISION SAFETY paragraph would otherwise describe a guard the function no longer has). (4) The paired QA leg uses the **corrupt-the-control idiom** (drop the constraint in a savepoint, seed the poison, assert NULL, roll back) — once the CHECK holds, the poison is un-seedable, and unreachable-by-construction is a reason to KEEP a leg, not skip it (`067`'s header rule).
- **Dependencies.** None upstream; Sec joint-review mandatory (financial-correctness constraint on the divisor of an inflation-adjusted figure).
- **Widened (Sec, SELF-221 joint-review 2026-08-13): the additive-never-replacement requirement now names BOTH fences.** `054` carries `nav_daily_value_finite` (NaN/±Infinity bar on `nav_value`), and it is what makes `071`'s `v_a_nav > 0` guard complete — same mechanism as `053`-behind-`067` (`NaN > 0` is TRUE). Two `> 0` guards in this family are sound only because a finiteness CHECK stands behind each; any future change to EITHER finiteness CHECK inherits condition (2) verbatim — additive, survives by name, never a replacement.
- **Dependent list extended (Sec, §2.1.4 backend joint-review 2026-08-14): `073 fn_nav_reference_dates` is the THIRD dependent of `053`'s `cpi_u_index_value_finite`** (after `067` and `071`) — its strictly-positive CPI guards are NaN/Infinity guards only because `053` bars them upstream. Any change to that CHECK now touches three consumers.

**Scratch-harness ACL parity (permissive-direction hazard).** [QA + DevOps]
- A scratch DB built with `pg_dump --no-privileges` loses the bootstrap's **REVOKEs as well as its grants** — measured 2026-08-12: `auth.uid()`'s `proacl` was NULL, EXECUTE defaulted to PUBLIC, making the harness MORE permissive than production, where denial assertions pass vacuously. Fix: mirror the bootstrap in both directions (grants present AND revokes present) in any scratch-verification harness; companion mechanism on record: schema `USAGE` is parse-time — RLS policy quals invoke by OID and need only EXECUTE, while test-typed `auth.uid()` needs `USAGE`, which is why product and test code diverge on the same harness.

**TBC fence scope widening over the `workers/etl` runner files.** [DevOps; fence-boundary change → F/CTO ratify + Sec review]
- Sec (SELF-217 review, measured): the TenantBoundConnection CI fence's production scope is `workers/etl/src/` and EXCLUDES the five root-level runner files, including the one SELF-217 added — no live violation, but the gap sits over exactly the hand-written-one-off class. Widen to `workers/etl/` minus `tests/`, with a paired inversion fixture.

**`.gitignore` + logging hygiene follow-through.** [DevOps]
- `*.log` added to `.gitignore` in this PR (no tracked `.log` existed); the SELF-217 importer already writes its full-figure run log inside the ignored `temp/nav-history/`. Residual: the log path is **cwd-relative** and `logging.FileHandler` raises if the directory is absent — recorded in the seeding-run preconditions on the Linear issue.

**`067` daily-grain cost magnitude post-import.** [Architect + PM]
- The imported decade moves `067`'s per-point helper loop from a handful of points to thousands at `'daily'` grain (~100× per request). Security property unchanged (the clamp still bounds to caller-owned rows) — this is an availability/product question. The per-distinct-month optimization is explicitly parked in `067`'s header with its revisit condition; the related staleness-rendering disposition rides the chart-UI issue per [ADR-053](DECISIONS.md#adr-053) Decision 7.

**`062` series-semantics documentation candidate.** [Architect; comment-only migration shape if adopted]
- The two landed-body properties that silently broke a carefully-built fixture — `062` never emits a period-end after the caller's newest checkpoint, and `'daily'` emits per calendar day, not per checkpoint — are now stated in the `067` battery's header where a fixture author will find them. Whether `062`'s own catalog comment should also carry them is open; if adopted, it is a `052`-shape comment-only migration.

**`workers/etl` packaging shape.** [Backend + DevOps]
- `pyproject.toml` has no `[build-system]` table and no `[tool.uv] package = true`, so `uv sync` treats the project as a dependency container and never installs `src/pfin_back_etl`; imports are invocation-dependent (`uv run --with-editable .` or `PYTHONPATH`), which cost one CI red on PR #445 (fixed file-locally with a location-derived bootstrap). Decide the durable packaging shape once, with the CI invocation as the reference environment.

### §7.15 — Phase-7 deploy-gate bookings (re-homed assertions)

*Assertions whose binding point is the Phase-7 deploy pass, re-homed from issue ACs so they survive issue closure. Tag convention `[PHASE-7 DEPLOY GATE]` per the §7.6 precedent; gates that arose as Sec follow-ups keep their §7.6 homes — one home per gate, no copies.*

**NAV-chart render-latency assertion (§2.1.2.d chart AC5 re-home).** [PHASE-7 DEPLOY GATE]
- **Source.** F/CTO ratify 2026-08-12 — Option A of the AC5 re-anchor. The original criterion pinned its measurement to Hetzner cax21, which is reference-only infrastructure per [ADR-021](DECISIONS.md#adr-021) greenfield (not the V1 deploy target); **the anchor moved, the budgets did not.** The chart issue's AC5 carries the matching re-homed text; this entry is the home that survives that issue's closure.
- **AC.** Measured on the **V1 deployment environment**, before the [PRD §2.1.2](docs/PRD/index.html#story-2-1-2) chart surface is declared deploy-complete: monthly-60-month default render within **800ms p95**; daily-90-day render within **1.5s p95**. The budgets are the ratified user-experience numbers and are **not re-litigatable here** — a miss on the deploy environment routes to F/CTO as a product decision (relax the budget vs optimize), never a silent re-anchor. QA's non-binding local-dev measurement (recorded at issue review) is early-warning evidence, not the binding measurement.
- **Dependencies.** **Blocked-by:** Phase 7 arrival — per ADR-021 greenfield the deployment environment does not exist until deploy, so there is nothing to measure before it. Owners: QA (measurement) + DevOps (environment) + PM (budget custody). **Deploy blocker for the chart surface, not a merge blocker** (the ratified deploy-blocker-not-merge-blocker shape).

**NAV-delta-panel latency assertion (§2.1.3.a backend AC6 re-home).** [PHASE-7 DEPLOY GATE]
- **Source.** F/CTO ratify 2026-08-13 — AC6 of the multi-horizon NAV-delta-panel reconciled AC set. The drafted criterion carried no measurement anchor (an unanchored latency number is not a testable acceptance criterion); re-homed per this section's ratified pattern. **The budget did not move — only the anchor.** The panel issue's AC6 carries the matching re-homed text; this entry is the home that survives that issue's closure.
- **AC.** Measured on the **V1 deployment environment**, before the [PRD §2.1.3](docs/PRD/index.html#story-2-1-3) panel surface is declared deploy-complete: `pfin.fn_nav_delta_panel()` returns within **200ms p95**. The budget is the ratified user-experience number and is **not re-litigatable here** — a miss on the deploy environment routes to F/CTO as a product decision (relax the budget vs optimize), never a silent re-anchor. QA's non-binding local-dev measurement (recorded at issue review) is early-warning evidence, not the binding measurement.
- **Dependencies.** **Blocked-by:** Phase 7 arrival — per [ADR-021](DECISIONS.md#adr-021) greenfield the deployment environment does not exist until deploy. Owners: QA (measurement) + DevOps (environment) + PM (budget custody). **Deploy blocker for the panel surface, not a merge blocker.**

### §7.16 — §2.1.3.b / §2.1.4 build follow-ups (2026-08-14)

*Recorded per the recorded-unless-runtime-effect rule; cited by title; no counts in this heading. Sources: the two Sec joint-reviews of this build day (072 AMBER→GREEN at `649ce8d`; 073 GREEN at `354e69f` carried to `cb7653f`), Architect/QA session findings. Detail lives in PRs #452 / #453 / #455 / #456 and the review threads.*

**`071` battery leak-canary subject change (family consistency).** [QA; battery-only]
- **Source.** Sec flag on both the 072 review (booked) and the 073 review (fixed on-branch for 073): a corrupt-the-control leg that probes `pfin.nav_daily` directly proves the policy fences the *table*, not that the *function's* read is fenced by nothing but that policy — it cannot catch a future redundant local `users_id` predicate (the `062`-measured hazard: such a predicate keeps a cross-tenant battery green over a policy broken open). `073`'s battery now aims its canary at the function itself; **`071`'s merged battery is the only family member still probing the table.**
- **AC (Sec's catch criterion, transcribed).** Change `071` `(LEAK1)`'s subject to a call of `pfin.fn_nav_delta_panel()` inside the broken-policy savepoint, keeping a fail-closed `ok(<val> is not null and <val> <> <A's own value>)` negative-predicate form (bare `isnt()` is IS DISTINCT FROM and passes on NULL — measured). The deterministic function-level assertion Sec sketched on the 072 review (anchor_checkpoint_date on the month row under sabotage) is an acceptable alternative shape.
- **Dependencies.** None; battery file is freely editable (not applied history). Pairs naturally with the next battery-touching PR on that surface.

**`072` header/catalog indexical defect (the "here" that re-points on copy).** [Architect; catalog half needs a `052`-shape comment-only migration]
- **Source.** Architect self-report 2026-08-14: `071`'s header sentence "struck for the FOURTH time in this family (049 R2 / 051 / 067 / here)" was carried verbatim into `072`, where the indexical "here" re-points — the enumeration silently omits `071` while still claiming "fourth". The catalog comment carries the same defect in shorter form. **New arrival route for the count-drift class: a stable count whose ENUMERATION moved because it contained an indexical — verbatim carry, the discipline that protects everything else, is what broke it.** `073` already avoids inheriting it (cites the enumeration, no ordinal).
- **AC.** File-header `--` half: correctable in place under the apply-migration in-place conditions at the next touch. Catalog half: has a database representation — fold the correction into the NEXT comment-touching migration on `pfin.fn_nav_delta_panel` (the `052` regenerate-and-diff shape) rather than spending a migration on it alone. Replacement shape: enumerate instances or cite `071`'s header; no ordinal.
- **Dependencies.** Rides the next `072`-object comment migration; no urgency (no runtime effect; the defect is a wrong count in prose).

**Scratch-DB recipe caveat: `pg_dump --schema-only` carries no role definitions.** [QA; runbook/DESIGN.md line]
- **Source.** Sec's C5 equivalence ruling on the 072 review: the schema-dump scratch recipe is sound on the SAME cluster (real `authenticated`/`anon`/`service_role` roles exist), but against a DIFFERENT cluster the ACL legs go vacuous or error — the dump carries no roles or memberships. Companion to §7.14's scratch-harness ACL-parity entry (that one is about `--no-privileges` dropping REVOKEs; this one is about roles not travelling at all).
- **AC.** One line in the QA battery-design doc (`tests/rls/DESIGN.md` when its §7.6 S9 entry lands, or wherever the scratch recipe is next written down): same-cluster only, or create the roles first.

**Battery-design lessons from this build day (candidates for the S9 `DESIGN.md` batch).** [QA]
- **pgTAP `isnt()` is fail-open on NULL** (IS DISTINCT FROM; `isnt(NULL, x)` passes) while `ok()` fails on NULL — a negative assertion over a subquery needs an explicit NULL guard, proven in three states (empty must fail / not-yet-detected must fail / detected must pass). Measured twice this session.
- **A suite whose fixtures derive from the run day needs legs exercised at synthetic run days** — the 073 battery's first draft carried a leg red for five months of the year, green only because it was August; the fix (override `fn_server_today` in scratch across 24+ dates incl. every month-end, then restore) needs no `p_as_of` parameter and does not touch the ratified no-client-clock design.
- **Fixture-vs-fixture disagreements are informative**: every Architect-vs-QA "contradiction" this session resolved into two different fixtures (seeded-vs-clean DB, pinned-vs-arrears CPI coverage, hardcoded-vs-file-built), not two readings of one fact — reproduce the OTHER party's exact fixture before contradicting their measurement.

### §7.19 — PRD + ARCH recalibration pass against the post-GL architecture (2026-08-16)

*Source: F/CTO direction 2026-08-16, verbatim: "In the future, we will need to recalibrate the items from the PRD, because we added the GL feature set and architecture partway through the design and *after* the PRD and ARCH docs were created." §7.6 framing note (1) applies — capture here; this is a standing hygiene item, not a milestone booking.*

**Systematic sweep of PRD §2 stories + ARCH sections against the GL-era schema — an amendment list per doc, not per-issue firefighting.** [PM lead; Architect co-owner for ARCH + design-conflict classification; owning agent per routed item]

- **Source.** The PRD and `docs/ARCH/index.html` were authored before the double-entry GL feature set landed (migrations ~030–039: transaction-type vocab/trade constraints, reclass_history, lot_match, journal, basis-adjust constraints, fn_gl_entries, GL completion, manual-trans/split write, stock-split entry) and before the account_trans/journal/lot_match substrate those migrations define. The measured symptom trail — four schema-impossible drafted-AC instances to date (SELF-223; SELF-229; SELF-228's `p_scope` reconciliation; SELF-234's supersession at the 2026-08-16 Option-C ruling) plus the five-issue V1.2 `p_users_id`/`p_scope` signature family caught in the 2026-08-15 PM reconciliation — is one pattern, not five accidents: **drafts written faithfully against doc text whose schema premises the GL era invalidated.** Per-issue AC-amendment passes fix instances; this entry retires the generator.
- **AC.**
  1. **PRD sweep** [PM]: every §2 story checked against the GL-era schema (migrations listing at execution time, read live — not the ~030–039 snapshot above). Output: a per-story amendment list, each conflict classified as (a) *text amendment* (PRD wording updated, no design change), (b) *ADR-worthy design conflict* (doc and schema genuinely disagree — routed to Architect for an options memo, F/CTO disposes), or (c) *already-superseded* (a later ADR/ratify resolved it — the PRD text gets the pointer, no re-litigation).
  2. **ARCH sweep** [Architect]: same triad classification over `docs/ARCH/index.html` sections against the same live schema baseline.
  3. **Drafted-but-unstarted issue ACs** [PM]: every AC in Linear-Backlog issues and §7 staged entries that copies a schema identifier is re-verified against DDL (the copied-not-composed rule) or marked SIGNATURE-PROVISIONAL; the sweep's baseline sha is recorded in the amendment list.
  4. **Routing discharged, not just listed**: each (a) lands as a doc amendment by its owning agent; each (b) gets an ADR-flag with owner named; each (c) gets its pointer edit. The pass is complete when the amendment list is empty or every residual is owned.
  5. Amendment lists are tracked artifacts (not `temp/`), one per doc, carrying the baseline sha.
- **Dependencies.** None blocking. **Standing trigger points:** (i) before each milestone-rotation promotion (the §7→Linear promotion inherits the sweep of the entries it promotes — AC 3 runs at minimum); (ii) before any PRD-anchored parity claim is asserted in a ratify or ship-gate. A full pass (AC 1+2) is F/CTO-scheduled; the trigger-point minimum (AC 3) is not optional at those boundaries.

### §7.20 — L1 cash-granularity ruling: landing residuals (2026-08-17)

*Source: F/CTO ruling 2026-08-17, verbatim: "Cash granularity = option (a), instrument-routed cash. Raw cash classifies once per currency into a bucket that must be labeled 'Cash' (or equivalent) — never FDIC/SPIC, because those names assert an insurance regime a catch-all doesn't honor. CD/T-Bill fill per-asset. Seed rows stay fillable, none pruned. (b) per-account classification stays V2." Ground truth: F/CTO inspected `Finance_Report_2026_04.pdf` p. 4 — the incumbent foots raw cash into ONE row, so the §3.3 impact is a label-mapping footnote, not a numeric carve-out. PRD §2.2.1/§2.2.2 body documentation landed with this entry; the §3.3 footnote was F/CTO-ratified and landed 2026-08-17. The ruling's "(or equivalent)" label was fixed as **"Cash Balances"** by F/CTO 2026-08-17 ("Cash Equivalents" was considered and rejected — the accounting term denotes exactly the instruments this bucket excludes).*

**1. Seed delta: a "Cash Balances" raw-cash Sub-Cat in `taxonomy_default`.** [Architect authors the migration; PM spec below]

- **Source.** The 041 seed's asset Cash Cat carries exactly four Sub-Cats (FDIC / SPIC / T-Bill / CD) — no raw-cash catch-all row ("Cash Balances" or otherwise) — and 009/041 make V1 taxonomy SEED-ONLY (no CRUD UI; the 041 INSERT path's sole V1 caller is the existence-guarded provisioning bootstrap). So the ruling's required bucket cannot exist for any user with zero seed change; a seed delta is REQUIRED. (DDL verified against 009/022/041/076 at `origin/main` = `e8e434a`.)
- **AC (what the delta must achieve — Architect owns the how).**
  1. One new row in `pfin.taxonomy_default`: `domain='asset'`, `cat='Cash'`, `sub_cat='Cash Balances'` (F/CTO-fixed 2026-08-17, superseding PM's `'Cash'` default), tax-neutral like the other asset rows, `display_order` slotting it first among the Cash rows, `notes` stating it is the per-currency raw-cash catch-all and asserts no insurance regime.
  2. The four existing Cash Sub-Cat rows are untouched (ruling: none pruned; §3.3 strict Sub-Cat-enumeration equality forbids pruning regardless).
  3. Reach: new users receive the row via the unchanged 041 provisioning statement. Already-provisioned users are NOT reached by first-access provisioning (041 existence-guard / GROWTH NOTE) — per 041, set-growth that must reach existing users is its own data-migration/backfill decision. Architect decides backfill-vs-`db reset` for the dev-only greenfield population and states the choice in the migration header.
  4. Vehicle: a data migration (041: `taxonomy_default` content is migration-authored; "changing the default set later is a data migration").
- **Dependencies.** None upstream. Downstream: SELF-238/240 AC drafting (item 2) does not block on the seed delta but the §2.2.2 Cash-row rendering semantics assume it lands before parity is asserted.

**2. SELF-238/240 AC-drafting note — what the cash / `Unsorted` rows mean under the ruling.** [PM, at AC drafting]

- Raw cash of non-liability account types (per 076 L1: one classification per user per currency via the currency-asset's single 022 junction row) foots into the "Cash Balances"-labeled Sub-Cat row of the §2.2.2 Cash Cat group once classified; FDIC / SPIC / T-Bill / CD rows carry per-asset-assigned holdings only. Liability-account cash is routed mechanically by `account_type` to the "Liability Balances" row of the Liabilities Cat group (F/CTO-ratified liability-routing package, 2026-08-17; realized at SELF-329) and never enters the per-currency classification. Unclassified non-liability raw cash surfaces in the derived `Unsorted` row (076 R2 emits the NULL-taxonomy row; SELF-238 renders it, never blocking per PRD §2.4.1). §2.2.3 (SELF-240, US Equity drill) carries no cash rows; the ruling's effect there is only that AC drafting no longer waits on the granularity question. The §7.19 AC-3 copied-not-composed rule applies to both drafts at drafting time.

### §7.21 — 054-battery refinements from the PR #485 Sec review (2026-08-17) — ✅ DISCHARGED at PR #488 (2026-08-17, rode the SELF-328 branch; Sec-verified)

*Source: Sec joint-review of `feature/cash-seed-and-kernel-gates` (PR #485, merged `3d6b538`) — two non-blocking Sec findings on `supabase/tests/rls/054_nav_daily_rls.sql`, explicitly kept OUT of the C1 closure so they get an owner. Owner: QA; no joint review needed (test-file text only, no assertion semantics change permitted beyond the named split).*

**1. h12/h18 failure messages must name the duplicate-grantor cause.** Both legs `string_agg` over `pg_auth_members` with no de-duplication — which is CORRECT and is exactly how they caught the 2026-08-17 doubled-membership drift (`authenticated,authenticated,service_role,service_role`). But h12's RED text says "RED if a THIRD membership were granted" and h18's says "RED on a re-grant WITH SET FALSE" — neither is what happened, and a reader chasing a nonexistent third role lands on the tempting repair `string_agg(distinct …)`, **which would make both legs permanently tolerate exactly the drift they just caught. `distinct` is FORBIDDEN in both legs** (Sec, verbatim intent: "The legs are correct; the environment is wrong"). AC: add a duplicate-grantor clause to both failure messages (cause = the same membership recorded twice under two grantors; fix = `REVOKE … GRANTED BY <grantor>`, never `distinct`), and add one line to the LEG INDEPENDENCE design note: its h18 scoping was designed against membership-SET drift and does not model grantor multiplicity, so one duplicate-grantor defect reddens both legs — a fence's independence argument is only as good as the drift dimensions it enumerated.

**2. h14 conjunction split.** The leg returns one boolean over `rolcanlogin = false AND rolpassword is null`, so a RED does not self-identify which half fired and the half-specific EXPECTED-DIFFERENT annotation (the `meta/battery-local-stack-disposition` ruling) has to send the reader to a manual query. AC: split into two assertions (plan 1→2) — `rolcanlogin = false` (never excused, any stack) and `rolpassword is null` (EXPECTED-DIFFERENT on a post-recovery cluster per the existing annotation, GREEN in CI). Assertion semantics unchanged; only the reporting granularity moves.

- **Dependencies.** None. Ride the next branch that touches `054` or the next QA battery PR, whichever comes first. The SELF-326 NEG1/NEG2 leg-text rider (name the issue ID in `079`'s battery) is booked on SELF-326 itself, not here.

### §7.22 — post-SELF-233/328 follow-ups (2026-08-17)

*Source: the SELF-233 and SELF-328 Sec joint reviews (PRs #488/#489). Three items, each with a named owner; none blocking; each returns to Sec per its own trigger.*

**1. `z.coerce.number()` on FK-shaped ID fields → strict typed parse.** [Backend] Sec-measured at the SELF-233 review: `z.coerce.number()` accepts `true`→1, `[5]`→5, `"0x10"`→16, `"5e2"`→500, `" 7 "`→7 — so in one `.strict()` schema `target_percent` rejects scientific notation by name while `sub_cat_id` accepts `"5e2"`. Pre-existing house pattern, SEVEN sites (schemas/planning-target.ts ×1, classification.ts ×2, transaction.ts ×4 — re-grep at fix time, don't trust this enumeration). No cross-tenant path (the 074 #17 trigger and RLS hold), but Lock-14 mod #1's literal text is "strict typed-input validation." Fix shape (Sec): `z.number().int().positive()` (no coerce), or an explicit string branch `z.string().regex(/^\d+$/).transform(Number)` where a form posts strings — a positive pin, not a widened coercion. One PR, all sites, Sec joint-review (Lock-14 surface).

**2. GOLDEN3 — in-file proof that FENCE1c bites.** [QA, at the next `078`-battery touch] Sec flag from the SELF-328 review, deliberately NOT a condition ("satisfying my conditions ends a review"). Shape supplied: a second savepoint-scoped mutation deriving from live prosrc — `regexp_replace(v_def, 'case ep\.source', 'CASE ep.source', 'g')` — asserting count(kernel_block)=2 while FENCE1a stays 3 and FENCE1b stays 1; completes the per-leg mutation matrix GOLDEN2 started and guards the "simplify count(kernel_block) to count(*)" rot path.

**3. RT-23 acceptance-text reconciliation to the post-ADR-056 shape.** [Sec — their doc] The RT-23 catalog entry's numeric-battery acceptance text still specifies a CURRENCY regex (`/^\$?[0-9]{1,12}(,[0-9]{3})*(\.[0-9]{1,2})?$/` — accepts `$` and thousands commas) for a surface that post-ADR-056 is `numeric(5,2)` percent with no currency field; the implementation is stricter and correct, but an auditor comparing build-vs-text reads a false gap. Sec drafts the replacement (offered at the SELF-233 review); lands as a SECURITY doc edit with its own diff-verify.

### §7.24 — GL-split ([ADR-058](DECISIONS.md#adr-058)) landing bookings (2026-08-18)

*Source: the F/CTO-directed taxonomy-vs-GL structural brainstorm (2026-08-18) and its Sec touchpoint, as recorded in [ADR-058](DECISIONS.md#adr-058). Each item is a finding that ADR produced and does not itself discharge; **the ADR's own follow-up inventory is the authoritative list** and this section is its §7 half. Numbering here is §7-local: the ADR inventory's items 4–8 map to items 1–5 below in order, and its item 3 (GL-native P&L) books to **§5.3** instead. Items the ADR deliberately does NOT book here: the rename PR's PM riders (they travel with that PR), the `009` stale `comment on table` correction (it rides this work's first migration, which re-emits that comment anyway), and the two build-spec open items (recorded inside the ADR itself). Closure annotations for **§7.13** and **§5.7** travel in the same landing PR.*

**1. The posting-template / `ledger_account` construct.** [decision item; Architect → its own ADR; Sec joint-review MANDATORY]
- **Source.** [ADR-058](DECISIONS.md#adr-058) Decision 1's ratified scope, F/CTO verbatim 2026-08-18: *"split only: follow on for template construction"*. `pfin.posting_prototype` is the **home** this construct will attach to; ADR-058 builds the home and not the construct.
- **AC.** Its own ADR carrying template header + legs + amount rules, with its own options round. It also carries the **`ledger_account` global-only** commitment — ADR-058 records the short answer (commit global-only permanently) as **out of scope and explicitly unruled by Sec**, and it must be **decided before that table ships**. ⚠ **A template that computes amounts is a small rules engine in the money path**, so Sec joint-review travels *with* the proposal rather than following it. Design inputs to carry in, named so they are not re-derived: ADR-058 Decision 8's two don't-foreclose constraints (a prototype must be able to name accounts that are **not permanent**; nothing may complicate `fn_gl_entries`' as-of parameterization), and the **short-position classification rule**, which ADR-058 settles **with** this ADR rather than before it.
- **Dependencies.** Upstream: the split migration (the table must exist) **and item 2 below, which is upstream of any template DDL**. Downstream: GL-native P&L (**§5.3**, V2).

**2. [ADR-031](DECISIONS.md#adr-031) Decision 3's event-class-vs-flow-class partition amendment.** [decision item; F/CTO ratify + Sec joint-review; **GATES the template stream**]
- **Source.** [ADR-058](DECISIONS.md#adr-058) Decision 1: making the Cat the authoritative class source for *every* row — rather than only for the single `standard` single-contra branch — would amend a **Sec-conditioned ratified decision** ([ADR-031](DECISIONS.md#adr-031) Decision 3 as amended, Amendment 1 item 2, which carries Sec Condition A binding on M1-evt). ADR-058 does not re-open it and **nothing in ADR-058 presumes its outcome**.
- **AC.** An F/CTO ratify pass with Sec joint-review, deciding whether the Category becomes the authoritative accounting-class source for every row or stays confined to the `standard` branch. ⚠ **There is NO MIGRATION for this to ride** — it is not a schema change, so it has no natural vehicle and will not surface on its own; it must be scheduled as its own decision item or it will be discovered by a template author assuming an answer. **It gates the template stream: it is upstream of any posting-template DDL**, because a template design that assumes the Cat is authoritative for every row has already presumed this outcome.
- **Dependencies.** Upstream: none — decidable today, ahead of all three migrations. Downstream: item 1 cannot author DDL ahead of it.

**3. Totals-equality watcher at the `fn_subcat_market_value` / `fn_compute_nav` seam.** [QA + Architect]
- **Source.** [ADR-058](DECISIONS.md#adr-058) Decision 3, consequences 3–5: the binding contract at the storage-taxonomy/GL seam is `Σ fn_subcat_market_value(as_of, include_real_estate := true) = fn_compute_nav(as_of)` (`076:38–40`), and it is **asserted and only HALF-WATCHED** — valuation *method* parity is watched structurally (`078` leg (S1); `079` V1–V3), but **no battery compares a `fn_subcat_market_value` total to a `fn_compute_nav` scalar**.
- **AC.** A battery leg asserting the totals equality. ⚠ **The named failure class is COVERAGE DIVERGENCE, not method divergence** — a row set and a denominator derived from different predicates, under-summing on a financial surface, failing **OPEN**. ADR-058 recommends it because the split, the `element` column and the rename each create a fresh coverage-divergence opportunity and this is the one seam all three land on. **Wrinkle to encode rather than to report later as a defect:** since the assets-only ruling the **rendered** §2.2.2 table deliberately does *not* foot to NAV (Liabilities are excluded from both row set and denominator; NAV includes them) — the invariant belongs to `fn_subcat_market_value` **as a whole**, not to what §2.2.2 renders.
- **Dependencies.** None — buildable today, and worth more standing *before* the three migrations than after.

**4. No repo-wide `relrowsecurity` watcher over `pfin` (Sec F8).** [DevOps + QA]
- **Source.** Sec touchpoint 2026-08-18, finding **F8**, routed to BACKLOG by Sec explicitly **not** as a gate on [ADR-058](DECISIONS.md#adr-058). `025`'s battery is per-table behavioural, not an enumerative sweep, so **a new `pfin` table shipped with a missing policy or a missing aal2 clause would be caught by review or not at all**.
- **AC.** A catalog sweep asserting `relrowsecurity` across every `pfin` relation, with an **explicit allowlist** for any relation deliberately exempt.
- **Dependencies.** None. ⚠ Its absence is *why* ADR-058 Decision 1 makes `posting_prototype`'s structural battery legs (the `074` S1/S2/S3a/S3b model) **non-optional** — those per-table legs are standing in for a watcher that does not exist, and building this one does not retire them.

**5. `080:174`'s `select distinct ut.users_id from pfin.user_taxonomy` becomes the wrong user set post-split (Sec F10).** [Backend / Architect, at the next seed delta]
- **Source.** Sec touchpoint 2026-08-18, finding **F10**. That derivation is [ADR-057](DECISIONS.md#adr-057)'s ratified backfill user-set shape; after the split it names the **storage-side** user set, so any future **prototype-side** seed backfill reaching for it delivers to the wrong population.
- **AC.** The next prototype-side seed delta derives its user set from `pfin.posting_prototype`, not from `pfin.user_taxonomy`; ADR-057's by-construction reasoning is re-applied per table rather than copied across tables. ⚠ **It fails CLOSED** — a user simply misses a category — which is the direction that does not announce itself, **and it is the idiom the next seed PR will copy**. That is why it is booked rather than left to the reviewer.
- **Dependencies.** Upstream: the split migration. Not actionable before it.

**6. [ADR-058](DECISIONS.md#adr-058) Amendment 1 — TWO corrections to Decision 7, and it does not exist yet.** [Architect; rides the split PR]
- **Source.** (i) F/CTO ratify 2026-08-19, the amended equality predicate; (ii) Sec joint-review finding **B3** on PR #502.
- **AC.** One appended Amendment 1 to ADR-058 carrying **both** corrections, so D7 is not amended twice:
  - **(i) The equality-assertion predicate.** D7's literal *"the database's asset-domain Cat set equals `CAT_GROUP_ORDER` exactly"* is **unsatisfiable as written** — the DB asset set has six members and `CAT_GROUP_ORDER` has five, because `Real Estate` is excluded by design (`076` excludes it at `p_include_real_estate := false`; `nonReAllocation.ts` filters it independently). Found independently by Architect and Backend. F/CTO's ratified replacement, transcribed: *"DB asset-Cat set MINUS `'Real Estate'` equals `CAT_GROUP_ORDER`, both directions, exclusion named and justified inside the assertion itself"* — a visible constant, never a silent filter. ⚠ **The decision to ship the assertion is UNCHANGED; only its stated predicate was loose.**
  - **(ii) The "all 11 others" figure.** `grep -c` counts LINES. Per occurrence: `028` = 3, `035` = 6, `037` = 6 → **15**, not 11. ⚠ **D7 itself warns, about the PRD count, that `grep -c` counts lines — and then does not apply the rule to its own migrations measurement.** `082`'s header inherited the figure faithfully and is corrected there; **fixing only the copy leaves the source still minting it.** The separation CLAIM is unaffected — Sec verified it per occurrence (`035:220/228/247/255`, `037:310/318/334/342`); nothing asset-domain hides in the 11-vs-15 gap. `041` is where the defect hides, because there lines and occurrences coincide, so the one file a reader spot-checks agrees with itself.
- **Dependencies.** Upstream: none — writable today. ⚠ **Sec's N3 warning, adapted: Amendment 1 does not exist yet and now carries BOTH corrections, so if the split PR slips, TWO known-wrong statements sit in the canonical record rather than one.** This is the entry on this list whose cost grows with delay.
- *[Annotation 2026-08-19 — **CLOSED.** [ADR-058](DECISIONS.md#adr-058) **Amendment 1** is appended and carries BOTH corrections, landing with the split PR (migration `084`) as this entry specified. Item 1's replacement predicate is transcribed from F/CTO's ratify verbatim; item 2's per-occurrence figures were **re-measured** at authoring rather than carried from this entry — `028` 3, `035` 6, `037` 6 — and agree with what is booked here. ⚠ Two collisions are recorded in the amendment so a later reader does not conflate them: `041`'s lines and occurrences coincide at 15, which is why the discrepancy hid; and `041`'s asset-domain figure (15) equals the corrected non-`041` occurrence total (15) by coincidence, counting different things.]*

**7. Venue env-var rename — `QA_SELF238_*` → venue-generic.** [QA + Backend; own commit, announced]
- **Source.** Architect decision at PR #502, taken and stated rather than deferred silently.
- **AC.** Rename `QA_SELF238_POSTGREST_URL` / `QA_SELF238_JWT_SECRET` to venue-generic names (e.g. `QA_PGRST_URL` / `QA_PGRST_JWT_SECRET`) across **both** server test files — `nonReAllocation.tenant-isolation.server.test.ts` and `nonReAllocation.catGroupOrderEquality.server.test.ts` — in its own commit, **announced to QA before landing**.
- **Why it was NOT done in the rename PR.** These vars configure a **throwaway Postgres+PostgREST venue**, not a fixture belonging to the issue they are named after; they are the established convention and one pair drives both tests. ⚠ Renaming them **changes an operator-facing procedure QA already has configured**, so doing it inside a PR about a different rename means a QA run can fail for a reason unrelated to what is under review. The misnomer has no runtime effect. **Mitigation already landed:** the new test file's header states the vars name a VENUE, so the name stops hardening while this booking waits.
- ⚠ **Rejected shape, recorded so it is not re-proposed:** venue-generic names with the old pair as a fallback — two sources of truth for one venue, and the fallback branch would be untested wherever the new names are set.
- **Dependencies.** None.

**8. `054`'s membership watcher re-shapes to EFFECTIVE PRIVILEGE — plus the live dual-grantor state it is currently detecting.** [QA + DevOps; F/CTO authorization for the remediation half]
- **Source.** Sec joint-review finding **F1** on PR #502, verbatim on the ruling: *"Urgency: LOW. Routing: book it; do not gate the rename on it."*
- **The measurement, taken on F/CTO's dev cluster and not relayed.** `pfin_etl` holds **each of its two ratified memberships TWICE, under two grantors** (`postgres` and `supabase_admin`) — four `pg_auth_members` rows where two are ratified. `054`'s legs `(h12)` and `(h18)` are RED locally because of it. Resolved against a **fresh** run after a report discrepancy Sec surfaced: **RED = `(h12)` / `(h14b)` / `(h18)`; GREEN = `(h13)` / `(h14a)` / `(h15)` / `(h16)` / `(h17)`.** `(h14b)` is that file's own documented EXPECTED-DIFFERENT-LOCALLY half and is unrelated. ⚠ **The NEVER-EXCUSED half `(h14a)` (`rolcanlogin = f`) is GREEN** — the role is inert, which is the leg that bounds the blast radius.
- **AC, two halves.**
  - **(a) The durable fix (Sec's).** Re-shape the assertion from *raw membership rows* to *effective privilege* (`pg_has_role` / the effective ACL set), so a benign duplicate grant cannot redden it. Sec's rationale, transcribed: duplicate grants add **no privilege** — they are idempotent in effect — and *"the real cost is watcher habituation: a leg that is permanently RED on F/CTO's cluster and GREEN in CI is a leg that gets excused, and the next time it goes red for a real reason nobody will look."*
  - **(b) The remediation.** Clear the duplicate grants with a **per-grantor** `REVOKE … GRANTED BY <grantor>` — never a `string_agg(distinct …)`-shaped tolerance in the leg, which would make the assertion permanently excuse the drift it just caught. ⚠ **Touches role grants on the dev cluster: F/CTO authorization required before anyone runs it.**
- ⚠ **Not PR-caused** — reproduces identically on a `001..081` control stack. **CI is unaffected** (fresh cluster). **LOW urgency; not a ship gate.**
- *[Annotation 2026-08-19, from the split PR (`084`) — **the fresh-cluster claim above is CORROBORATED, not falsified**, and this annotation exists because it was briefly reported as falsified. Recorded in full because the retraction is the reusable part.* **What happened:** a full pgTAP run against a scratch database carrying `001..084` reproduced these three legs RED (25, 28–29), and a `001..083` control with unpatched tests reproduced them **identically** — so they are confirmed **not** `084`-caused, which is the half that stands. **They were then reported as evidence that "CI is unaffected (fresh cluster)" was wrong. That inference was incorrect.** ⚠ **The scratch recipe builds a fresh DATABASE on the SHARED CLUSTER, and `pg_auth_members` is a CLUSTER-LEVEL catalog** — role memberships do not live in a database and do not travel in a per-database dump, which is the very reason the recipe puts the scratch on the same cluster (so the real `authenticated` / `anon` / `service_role` roles exist). Measured at the time of writing: `pfin_etl` holds **four** membership rows — `authenticated` and `service_role`, each granted twice, by `supabase_admin` and by `postgres`. **Every scratch DB on that cluster inherits that state, so no scratch on it can ever serve as a control for a cluster-level assertion.** CI spins a genuinely fresh *cluster* and the `DB tests (pgTAP RLS battery)` lane was verified **green on `main` at `9e895a4`** on the same day, which is exactly what this entry predicts. ⚠ **One nearby observation eliminated so it is not later mistaken for flakiness in these legs:** the same lane shows a `failure` on `main` at `386a65b`, three minutes earlier — inspected, and it is a Docker **port-bind collision** (`failed to bind host port for 0.0.0.0:54322: address already in use`) from two runs racing, not a test result. **Method rule this yields, which is the durable output:** *a scratch database is a control for schema-level and data-level claims only; for anything reading `pg_auth_members`, `pg_roles`, or grantor identity, the control has to be a fresh CLUSTER, and a same-cluster scratch will silently reproduce the very drift it is supposed to be isolating.* Resolution of these three legs stays with **(a) the effective-privilege re-shape** above — that work has to understand the failure in order to re-shape the assertion, and this annotation narrows what it must explain: not "why does CI differ from local" but "why does a cluster-level duplicate grant redden a leg that measures privilege."]*
- **Dependencies.** None. (a) and (b) are independent; (a) is worth doing even if (b) never runs.

**9. Occurrence sweeps must state their quoting coverage AND verify the path exists.** [method guidance; Architect + whoever authors the next sweep]
- **Source.** Architect flag at PR #502, **closed for this rename by Backend's re-sweep** — booked as guidance, not as open work on the rename.
- **What happened, and it is two failure modes rather than one.** ADR-058 Decision 7 records consumer reach as *"`workers` **0**"* and *"`web/src` **0**"*, both from a **single-quoted** `'Equity'` grep.
  - **`workers`** — Python source uses double quotes. Three `== "Equity"` comparisons exist in the folded ETL. Re-measured: all three bind to `pfin.asset_cat`, a table **absent from V1** (no `create table` for it anywhere in `supabase/migrations/`; the only bare occurrences there are prose references to the incumbent). **Verdict unchanged — NOT AFFECTED — but for the reason *"different system"*, not *"wrong label"*, and the measurement had never been exercised against Python's quoting.**
  - **`web/src`** — Backend's finding, verified independently against the tree: **that directory does not exist**, having been folded into `api/src` earlier. ⚠ **A sweep over a path that does not exist returns 0 and reads exactly like evidence of absence.** Backend covered what "web/src" now means with a **case-insensitive** re-sweep across all of `api/src`, which found nothing beyond the files already in the PR.
- **AC.** A sweep that reports a count states (i) its **quoting coverage** and (ii) that its **paths resolve**. A `0` from an unresolved path is not a finding.
- **Dependencies.** None.

**10. Split-PR sweep riders — stale `'Equity'` fixture rows and one stale comment.** [QA + Backend; rides the split PR]
- **Source.** Sec joint-review findings **F2** and **N2** on PR #502, both explicitly non-blocking there.
- **AC.**
  - The `('asset','Equity', …)` **fixture rows** still seeded by the `076` / `077` / `078` / `080` batteries. These are **user-authored rows, not seeded ones**, so nothing breaks today and no leg is wrong — but they carry a retired label into every future read of those files.
  - `api/src/lib/server/queries/subcatMarketValue.ts:12` — *"§2.2.3 is a pure Equity drill-down"*. Comment only, no runtime effect, no fail-open. Sec's reason for recording it, transcribed: *"removing this exact ambiguity from the vocabulary is Decision 7's stated purpose, and the split/`element` PRs will be read against it."*
- **Dependencies.** Rides the split PR's sweep. Not urgent alone.

**11. The `fn_gl_entries` reachability finding has a TRACKED home — do not re-derive it.** [pointer entry; no work]
- **Source.** Sec joint-review finding **B1** on PR #502 (the AMBER blocking condition), discharged as header text.
- **Where it lives.** `supabase/migrations/082_taxonomy_rename_marketable_securities.sql`, the **ONE DERIVED OUTPUT DOES CHANGE** block. Tracked, versioned, and read by exactly the person who needs it.
- **The substance, in one line so this entry is searchable.** `fn_gl_entries` joins `pfin.user_taxonomy` with **no domain predicate** (`037:225` annotation, `037:349` split-child), and `023` / `029` each record in their own DOMAIN NOTE that matched-DOMAIN is **app-layer only** in V1 — so an asset-domain row is a permissible `sub_cat_id` target at the DB layer, and the rename moved its emitted contra from the Equity branch to Suspense. **Fail-safe in this direction.** ⚠ **The reusable half is the REVERSE direction: an asset Cat renamed INTO a cashflow-class name would traverse the identical unfenced join and mis-post SILENTLY.** [ADR-058](DECISIONS.md#adr-058) Decision 1 closes it structurally once `023` / `029` re-target to `pfin.posting_prototype`.
- **AC.** None — this entry exists so the analysis is findable from the backlog after the review document is gone. **Per Sec, no battery leg is owed in the rename PR;** any leg belongs with the split PR, where the hazard is resolved by construction rather than narrowed.

**12. Sec F2's four row counts RE-RUN at the first populated instance — a precondition on the incumbent import, not a discharged fact.** [Architect + Backend; precondition, gates the import]
- **Source.** Sec touchpoint 2026-08-18, finding **F2**, as discharged at migration `084` (2026-08-19). The counts were run before authoring, as F2 required, and **all four came back zero with all four denominators also zero** — every referent table was empty, because no production-shaped V1 dataset exists (greenfield; no V1 deployment; the incumbent is reference-only and is not queried). F2's own wording asks for *"production-shaped data rather than a fresh stack"*, and with respect to those four tables the most-populated V1 instance in existence **is** a fresh stack.
- **AC.** Before the first import that populates `account_trans_annotation`, `account_trans_split`, `user_asset_category` or `account_trans_annotation_history`, **re-run all four counts with F2's predicates**, record them with their venue, and apply F2's rule unchanged: **any non-zero count gets an F/CTO disposition first.** ⚠ **Counts 1–3 fail CLOSED** — every live FK is `on delete restrict`, so a violation aborts rather than corrupts — which means the risk `084` faced was never retired by its zero, only **deferred to whichever instance first has rows**. ⚠ **Count 4 fails SILENTLY**: nothing constrains it, and a non-zero result there is what would make [ADR-058](DECISIONS.md#adr-058) Decision 2's id-preservation invariant demonstrably load-bearing rather than structurally argued. That invariant is ratified on the hazard's structure and **remains empirically undemonstrated** — supported by neither `084`'s zero-over-empty nor by anything else yet.
- **Why booked rather than left in the migration header.** `084`'s header carries the counts, their venue, their vacuity and this re-run condition. **A precondition that lives only in a migration header is discharged by whoever does not read it** — and the reader who needs this one is writing an importer, not reading a year-old migration.
- **Dependencies.** Upstream: none. Triggered by the first import that lands rows in any of the four tables.

**13. `085`'s battery TAP tail carries no REF and no CONSUMER.** [QA; rides their next edit to `supabase/tests/rls/085_taxonomy_element_rls.sql` — next-touch rider, not standalone work]
- **Source.** Sec's merge-gate remediation (A) on the ELEMENT PR (PR #510) asked for the raw TAP tail as an observation settling the plan-drift question. It is pasted in BLOCK C **unlabelled**. QA authored a REF/CONSUMER label after the commit had landed, and it named the sha *before* the correction the file itself carries — the sound-quote/false-gloss shape — so it was not committed. Sec's requirement is satisfied by PR #510's body, which names both; this entry is durability — the body is not in the tree; the file is.
- **AC (four clauses; texts per the Architect/QA exchange of 2026-08-20).** At the next edit to that file, label the tail with: **(1) REF** — the sha the run exercised; **(2) CONSUMER** — containerized `pg_prove` with image tag, never bare `psql`; **(3) a note that the annotation postdates the run it describes** — any label added in a later commit is necessarily about an earlier ref, and saying so is what keeps it honest rather than merely accurate; **(4) paste a tail's SHAPE only** (plan line, `ok` sequence, trailing `#` comment, `Result: PASS`) **and elide description text ON PURPOSE, every time** — a description pasted into the file it describes goes stale the instant that description is next edited; the `085` tail was safe **by accident** (its elision fell before the point where `(B5)`'s old and new wordings diverge, author-confirmed elision-for-width, not intent). A truncated tail is evidence of the shape, not of exact strings — do not over-read it.
- **Dependencies.** None. Do not open a commit for it alone.
- *[Annotation 2026-08-20, from the SELF-242 arc — one more thing to settle at that same next-touch edit: an AGGREGATE `pg_prove` run over the full `/tests` tree reported this file as "planned 19 tests but ran 18" while still printing `Result: PASS`; the isolated run of the same file shows 19/19 with every `ok` line present. Observed independently by QA and Backend the same day, on different scratch builds; unexplained — possibly a TAP-parser artifact of the tail's deliberate truncation (clause 4 above), which would make it THIS ITEM's own elision biting its consumer. The reader at risk is whoever trusts the aggregate run's plan accounting as the completeness check — which is precisely the job a TAP-aware consumer exists to do, so "PASS anyway" must not be normalized. Diagnose at the next-touch edit; if clause 4's shape-only paste is the cause, the fix belongs in how the tail is labeled, not in loosening the consumer.]*

**14. The element-aware equality watcher's residual — no assertion that every `taxonomy_default` Cat is rendered, named-excluded, or genuinely liability-element.** [QA + Architect; belongs with the SEED layer, at the next seed delta or battery extension — NOT with the §2.2.2 assertion]
- **Source.** [ADR-058](DECISIONS.md#adr-058) Amendment 2 (the second evolution of the Decision-7 equality predicate, landed with the §2.2.2 assets-only rework): the DB-side comparison set now filters `element='asset'` BEFORE comparing, so **a new asset-natured Cat mistakenly seeded `element='liability'` escapes the reverse leg entirely** — the pre-amendment form was total over the Cat vocabulary and would have failed on it. Recorded there as a trade, not a strengthening; this entry is the trade's watcher half.
- **AC.** A battery leg at the seed layer asserting the three-way partition is TOTAL: every distinct Cat in `pfin.taxonomy_default` is exactly one of (a) rendered — a `CAT_GROUP_ORDER` member, (b) named-excluded — the visible-exclusion constants of the §2.2.2 assertion, or (c) genuinely liability-element — ALL its rows carry `element='liability'`. A Cat matching none of the three (or a mixed-element Cat) fails the leg. ⚠ It must live with the SEED, not inside the §2.2.2 assertion — folding it into that assertion re-creates the very row-set/denominator coverage-divergence the amendment's predicate alignment exists to foreclose.
- **Dependencies.** None — buildable as soon as the amendment lands. Worth more standing BEFORE the next seed delta than after it.
